#2876. Top Wall Street analysts are bullish on the growth potential of these 3 stocks

Fears around the artificial intelligence trade and higher pressure on oil prices are weighing on global markets, but a few stocks offer investors solid growth prospects even in choppy times.

Investors with a long-term horizon can look beyond the ongoing volatility and track the recommendations of top Wall Street analysts to pick attractive stocks with strong long-term growth potential.

Here are three stocks favored by some of Wall Street's top pros, according to TipRanks, a platform that ranks analysts based on their past performance.

Health insurer UnitedHealth delivered better-than-expected results for the third quarter of 2026. The company also raised its full-year earnings outlook, reflecting continued progress on its turnaround plan.

Following the Q3 print, Morgan Stanley analyst Erin Wright reiterated a buy rating on UNH stock with a price target of $529. "UNH's beat & raise reinforces turnaround momentum, with improving MA [Medicare Advantage]/Optum Health supporting a higher '26 EPS baseline & upside to '27 consensus," said Wright.

The five-star analyst added that better-than-expected performance in the UnitedHealthcare and Optum Health businesses supported another upgrade to the company's 2026 adjusted earnings per share outlook to at least $20.50. Wright noted that UNH raised the earnings guidance despite incremental investments planned for the fourth quarter of 2026.

Furthermore, Wright highlighted management's view that the consensus 2027 EPS of $22.63 was too conservative. Reaching even the low end of the company's 13% to 16% long-term EPS growth target would imply an EPS of $23.17.

Wright raised her 2026 EPS estimate to $20.65 from $19.82 and 2027 EPS to $23.24 from $22.54, reflecting the latest upside and an improving 2026 medical loss ratio to 87.4% from 88.1%. Overall, she believes that UNH's operational performance continues to improve, increasing her confidence in its UHC strategy and Optum Health turnaround execution.

Wright ranks No. 264 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. Her ratings have been successful 64% of the time, delivering an average return of 15%. See UnitedHealth Ownership Structure on TipRanks.

Social media giant Meta Platforms has been making headlines since the launch of its Muse AI agent, which is seeing solid traction. Investors look forward to updates on the company's AI strategy when it announces its third-quarter results on Oct. 21.

Heading into Q3 earnings, Guggenheim analyst Michael Morris maintained a buy rating on Meta stock and increased his price target to $850 from $700, reflecting higher estimates for 2027. He expects Muse usage momentum and engagement levels to shape investor sentiment into the Q3 earnings call.

Specifically, Morris highlighted that, according to Apptopia, Muse reached 1.3 million daily active users in five weeks versus 300,000 in the first week. Given that Muse's current adoption represents less than 0.05% of Meta's 3.6 billion daily active user base, the analyst expects management to prioritize boosting usage over near-term monetization.

"Over time, we believe Muse monetizes primarily through businesses paying to reach consumers through their agents, consistent with the economics of Meta's core advertising business, rather than consumer wallets," said the five-star analyst. He added that the checkout integrations with Shopify and PayPal reflect this framework.

While there are concerns that a rival could overtake Muse or the recently launched Muse Wonder and push Meta into a prolonged cycle of overinvestment, Morris remains bullish due to the company's strong record in scaling WhatsApp and competing strongly with TikTok via Reels as well as free distribution to an extensive user base.

Morris ranks No. 629 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been successful 57% of the time, delivering an average return of 13.2%. See Meta Platforms KPIs on TipRanks.

Next is semiconductor company Marvell Technology. Earlier this month, Marvell held its Investor Day, where it raised its total addressable market estimate to $400 billion by 2030, reflecting solid AI-led demand tailwinds.

After hosting a non-deal roadshow with Marvell's senior management following the Investor Day, Stifel analyst Tore Svanberg reiterated a buy rating on the stock with a price target of $370.

The five-star analyst said that a major focus of the roadshow was the XPU and XPU Attach mix, as Marvell's Custom business expands toward its FY31 revenue target of $30 billion. In this regard, management reiterated that it expects a balanced mix of XPU and XPU Attach revenue in FY31, though it noted that either segment could grow rapidly enough to shift the mix toward 60/40 or 40/60.

While XPU drove most of the nearly $1.5 billion Custom revenue in FY26, Svanberg expects Marvell's XPU Attach business to grow substantially through the end of the decade, leading to a more balanced mix in this business. At the Investor Day event, the company raised its FY29 Custom target from more than $10 billion to over $12 billion, mainly driven by strength in the XPU Attach business.

Svanberg said that another key topic in the roadshow was whether Marvell can secure enough capacity to meet its raised targets. Management said that it met with suppliers ahead of Investor Day to share its five-year goals and received commitments to support these targets. The analyst believes that Marvell's supply chain commitments give it a competitive edge over rivals as customers seek reliable partners for large-scale infrastructure buildouts.

Svanberg ranks No. 91 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 58% of the time, delivering an average return of 31.8%. See Marvell Technology Statistics on TipRanks.

#2864. Top Wall Street analysts prefer these 3 dividend stocks for stable income

Global stock markets remain volatile amid persistent geopolitical tensions and concerns about the sustainability of AI trade. Given this backdrop, investors seeking steady income can add dividend-paying stocks to their portfolios.

Tracking recommendations from top Wall Street analysts can help investors pick attractive stocks, as these experts assess a company's ability to pay dividends consistently.

Here are three dividend-paying stocks that are highlighted by Wall Street's top pros, as tracked by TipRanks, a platform that ranks analysts based on their past performance.

Integrated oil and gas giant Chevron (CVX) is a dividend king. The company has increased its dividend for 39 consecutive years. At a quarterly dividend of $1.78 per share (annualized dividend of $7.12 per share), CVX offers a yield of 3.14%. Chevron will announce its third-quarter results on October 30.

In an earnings preview note on U.S. oil majors, Goldman Sachs analyst Neil Mehta reiterated a buy rating on Chevron with a price target of $241. The analyst is focused on the company's international growth prospects across Latin America, the Middle East, West Africa, and the Eastern Mediterranean.

Specifically, Mehta highlighted management's commentary on solid execution in Venezuela, with gross production expected to more than double to nearly 600 kbd (kilo barrels per day) by 2031 via three joint ventures. He also noted a recent oil and gas condensate discovery in offshore Angola and is awaiting commercial updates on the West Qurna 2 and Nasiriyah projects in Iraq.

Furthermore, Mehta expects CVX to report improved productivity and capital efficiency across domestic shale assets. He expects the improvement mainly in the Permian Basin, where Chevron projects advanced chemical treatments to drive about a 10% recovery uplift in new wells and bring down capital spend per barrel by about 25% year-over-year. Overall, Mehta expects Chevron to report Q3 2026 EPS of $4.95, above the consensus of $4.89.

"While unfavorable timing effects and volatile commodity prices may present quarterly earnings headwinds, we highlight healthy U.S. refining margins and strong asset utilization as partial offsets," said Mehta.

Mehta ranks No. 381 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 63% of the time, delivering an average return of 12%. See Chevron KPIs on TipRanks.

Kinetik Holdings (KNTK) is a fully integrated, pure-play, midstream company operating in the Delaware Basin. Earlier this month, the company declared a dividend of $0.81 per share, payable on October 30, 2026. With an annualized dividend of $3.24 per share, KNTK offers a yield of 5.82%. Kinetik will announce its Q3 earnings on November 4, with a conference call scheduled the next day.

Ahead of Q3 2026 earnings, Goldman Sachs analyst John Mackay reiterated a buy rating on Kinetik stock and slightly raised his price target to $58 from $57. He expects the company to report Q3 2026 EBITDA of $279 million, higher than the Street's expectation of $270 million but lower than the second quarter's EBITDA of $281 million.

The 5-star analyst expects Kinetik's Q3 performance to gain from higher gas volumes after notable curtailments in the second quarter. Mackay also expects the quarter to benefit from higher production and steady commodity margins, even though some lower-margin contracts and weaker Waha spread marketing gains could weigh on results.

Overall, Mackay increased his Q3 2026 estimates to reflect higher volumes, driven by rapid Permian egress pipeline commissioning, stronger throughput at Kings Landing I, and higher M2M commodity prices, offset by increased costs.

"As natural gas takeaway constraints ease across the Permian, investor focus centers on KNTK's YE26 volume trajectory and durability of its multi-year growth," said Mackay.

Mackay ranks No. 569 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 64% of the time, delivering an average return of 16.9%. See Kinetik's Ownership Structure on TipRanks.

Moving to Crescent Energy (CRGY), an exploration and production company with activities focused in the Eagle Ford, Permian, and Uinta Basins. With a quarterly dividend of $0.12 per share (annualized dividend of $0.48 per share), CRGY's dividend yield stands at 3.56%. Earlier this month, Crescent announced an agreement to acquire Eagle Ford assets from Devon Energy (DVN).

Heading into Q3 earnings on Nov. 2, Evercore analyst Stephen Richardson reiterated a buy rating on Crescent stock and slightly lowered the price target to $17 from $18, citing dilution from new shares.

"CRGY's recent acquisition of DVN's Eagle Ford assets marks a strategic, growth-oriented move, potentially enhancing cash flow performance," said Richardson.

The 5-star analyst noted that the acquisition of the Eagle Ford assets at a net purchase price of $3.85 billion will add about 68 MBoe/d of net production and over 600 net Tier 1 locations. Richardson added that a solid track record of synergies from past deals in the Eagle Ford region increases confidence about Crescent achieving efficiencies with this deal.

Additionally, Richardson noted that the financial implications of the acquisition seem impressive, with a projected increase in cash flow per share of about 9% for 2027 to 2029.

The analyst believes that Crescent is not just expanding its business through acquisitions but positioning itself as a more durable company, regardless of market conditions.

Richardson ranks No. 478 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 63% of the time, delivering an average return of 13.2%. See Crescent Energy Statistics on TipRanks.

#2863. 2 Ways Anthropic's IPO Pays Off for Alphabet Investors

A large equity stake and nearly guaranteed cloud sales make Anthropic's future success a boon for Alphabet.

By Chris Neiger – Nov 26, 2026 at 12:00AM EST

10 stocks we like better than Anthropic ›

Anthropic (ANT +1.08%) recently completed the largest initial public offering (IPO) in history, raising $81.3 billion -- beating the previous record set by SpaceX by $6.3 billion in June.

Many investors were no doubt pleased with Anthropic's public debut, but individual retail investors aren't the only ones to benefit. Alphabet (GOOGL -2.13%) (GOOG -2.02%) holds nearly 14% of Anthropic's shares, making it one of the largest shareholders in the artificial intelligence (AI) company.

What's more, Alphabet has significant cloud computing and processor deals with Anthropic, further entangling the two companies' futures. Here's how Alphabet and its shareholders benefit from the recent Anthropic IPO.

Starting in 2023, Alphabet made a series of investments in Anthropic over multiple funding rounds that eventually totalled about $33 billion. At the time of Anthropic's IPO, Alphabet held 13.7% of Anthropic's stock.

That investment was a very wise move, considering that Anthropic's shares were priced at $83 at the IPO, making Alphabet's position worth about $149 billion. Of course, as Anthropic's shares rise and fall, the value of Alphabet's stake shifts.

Holding Anthropic's shares boosts Alphabet's reported net income on paper each quarter (if Anthropic's returns go up), without generating actual cash. Alphabet could eventually sell some of its Anthropic shares to reinvest in the company, but it's likely to hold on to them, or at least the bulk of them, for a while.

If Alphabet holds onto its stake, it could add significant unrealized gains to its reported income. When it sells some of its shares, it will directly benefit shareholders by providing fresh capital for growth or buybacks. So, even though Alphabet and Anthropic compete with their AI models, Alphabet shareholders will still benefit as Anthropic grows.

Anthropic said in its S-1 filing before going public that it had about $121 billion in spending committed to Google between April 2026 and July 2033. The good news for Alphabet was that Anthropic was locked into that spending "regardless of actual usage."

What's more, Anthropic also buys Google's tensor processing units (TPUs) for AI processing. The AI company will reportedly spend $2.5 billion on Google's TPUs this year and up to $7.2 billion next year.

Altogether, Anthropic is estimated to spend up to $200 billion on Google Cloud and TPUs over the next five years.

With Anthropic being a core Google Cloud and TPU customer, its financial stability is a good thing for Alphabet. Now that Anthropic has raised more than $81 billion from its IPO, the deals between the companies are essentially de-risked because it can use the proceeds to pay for some of its commitments.

And Anthropic could expand its deals with Alphabet in the future, as demand for its services increase. Anthropic's third-quarter revenue rose 47% to $17 billion, indicating that the company is already on the right growth trajectory.

Alphabet is positioning itself well in the AI era as the company expands sales of its TPUs and grows its Google Cloud service. Morgan Stanley estimates that Google could generate $84 billion in graphics processing unit (GPU)-related sales next year alone. That would be an enormous opportunity for the company, considering that it only just began selling TPUs to customers earlier this year.

What's more, the company's Google Cloud revenue is rising fast and more than doubled in Q3 to $30.7 billion. The company ended the quarter with $561 billion in Google Cloud backlogs, 55% of which management says will be fulfilled over the next 24 months.

While Alphabet certainly benefits from its significant stake in Anthropic, I think the company's growth from Google Cloud and massive opportunity from selling TPUs make it a compelling AI stock on its own.

And given that Alphabet's shares trade at just 15 times the company's trailing earnings -- compared to the tech sector average of about 24 -- the company's stock looks like a good buy right now.

#2859. FinVolution (FINV) Q3 2026 Earnings Call Transcript

Loan volume fell as China funding tightened, but overseas growth held firm.

By Motley Fool Transcribing – Nov 26, 2026 at 12:08AM EST

Need a quote from a Motley Fool analyst? Email [email protected]

FinVolution (FINV -2.02%) reported a sequential contraction in loan volume as management prioritized asset quality and funding stability amid regulatory and institutional shifts in the Chinese market. The company stated that its diversification strategy successfully used growing international operations to offset domestic headwinds, with overseas revenue increasing to 37% of the total mix. In China, management responded to a tightening institutional funding market and reduced collection capacity by restricting credit to high-quality existing borrowers and upgrading risk models. Operational progress continued in international markets, including Indonesia and Australia, where the company expanded its offline presence and utilized open banking data to refine credit pricing. FinVolution maintained a liquidity position of RMB 8.4 billion while continuing to return capital through share repurchases and the retirement of convertible debt.

Operator: Thank you for participating in the Third Quarter 2026 Earnings Conference Call for FinVolution Group. [Operator Instructions] Today's conference call is being recorded. I'll now turn the call over to your host, Yam Cheng, Head of Capital Markets of the company. Yam, please go ahead.

Yam Cheng: Thank you, [ Desmond ]. Hi, everyone. Thank you for joining our call today, and welcome to our third quarter 2026 earnings. The company's results were issued via Newswire services earlier today and are posted online. You can download the earnings release and sign up for the company e-mail alerts by visiting the IR section of our company website. Mr. Tim Li, our CEO; and Mr. Alexis Xu, our CFO, will start the call with the prepared remarks and conclude with a Q&A section. During this call, we will be referring to several non-GAAP financial measures to review and assess our operating performance.

These non-GAAP financial measures are not intended to be considered in isolation or as a substitute for the financial information prepared and presented in accordance with U.S. GAAP. For information about these non-GAAP measures and reconciliation to GAAP measures, please refer to our earnings press release. Before we continue, please note that today's discussion will contain forward-looking statements made under the safe harbor provisions of the U.S. Private Securities Litigation Reform Act of 1995. Forward-looking statements involve inherent risks and uncertainties. As such, the company's results may be materially different from the views expressed today. Further information regarding these and other risks and uncertainties are included in the company's filings with the U.S. Securities and Exchange Commission.

The company does not assume any obligation to update any forward-looking statements, except as required under applicable law. Finally, we have also posted our results presentation on our website. I'll now turn over to our CEO, Tim. Please go ahead, Tim.

Tiezheng Li: Thanks, Yam. Welcome to our third quarter 2024 (sic) [ 2026 ] earnings call. Some strategies are built for comfort. Ours was built for quarters selective. For nearly a decade, we have spread our business across markets, economies, and regulatory regimes not because anyone of them was under pressure, but because we knew that one day, one of them could be. This quarter, that day came in China and while our domestic business absorbed the shock, our overseas business kept growing and kept they growing. Once again, diversification carried us. The third quarter was a challenging transition period for China business.

In July, a credit event at another platform eroded confidence across China's loan facilitation industry and financial institutions sharply put back funding to the sector. From late July, a nationwide campaign against collection agencies cut collection capacity and efficiency. Together, these 2 developments hit our China business on 3 fronts: advance funding supply, credit performance, and loan origination. We responded as we said we would on last quarter's call, prioritizing funding stability and asset quality over growth. We tightened underwriting and directed to funding available to us towards our best quality existing borrowers. As a result, group's loan volume contracted to approximately RMB 20 billion, down 56% sequentially, driven mainly by the slowdown in China.

Meanwhile, our diversification strategy continued to work as intended. Overseas volume grew 11% year-over-year to RMB 4 billion. Revenue rose to RMB 949 million, 11% year-over-year and 2% sequentially. Overseas now account for 37% of group revenue. More importantly, this growth is also profitable. Overseas operating profit more than doubled year-over-year to RMB 62 million. Now let me take each segment in turn, starting with Chinese Mainland. Regulation now reaches into every part of the lending process. The new financing cost disclosure rules took effect on August 1 and the new online marketing rules on September 30.

Compliance is our first priority, and we have worked with our bank partners on loan products and the referral flows that meet the new requirements. China loan volume contracted to approximately RMB 16 billion, down 61% sequentially, reflecting tighter funding supply and the elevated credit risk across the industry. On funding, institution turned risk averse from July and the supply fell sharply. Our priority throughout the quarter was to restore funding and keep it aligned with loan origination. We gave our bank partners full visibility into our fund flows and processes, and adopted our partnership model to their requirements and risk appetite, including closed-loop payment controls and additional checks.

As a result, funding supply recovered steadily from its July low through August and September. Though it remains below normal level in early October, we and other lending platform also completed compliance self-checks on fund flows and submitted clean audit reports from independent third-party firms to the National Internet Finance Association of China. We believe this marks an important step in restoring institutional confidence across the industry and our compliance record positioned us well as funding partners become more selective. On risk, our metrics improved steadily throughout the first half of 2026.

The trend reversed in July, driven by 2 industry-wide factors: First, as funding tightened across the industry, borrowers had less access to credit and the repayment to legacy loans generally weakened; Second, the collection campaign we flagged last quarter reduced recovery capacity and pushed the delinquencies higher. In response, we upgraded our risk models and tightened underwriting further during the quarter. Our approach was simple, lend less, lend it to the people we know, and keep bad actors out. I would like to further elaborate on this in a bit. In this elevated risk environment, we expect legacy loans to continue to weigh on credit costs.

Looking ahead, we will maintain our compliance-first risk-focused approach through this reset in China with asset quality as the core priority. Now let's move on to the overseas segment. Our overseas business maintained its growth momentum with volume up 5% sequentially to RMB 4 billion. Our diversification markets portfolio continued to act as a buffer against country-specific headwinds. In Indonesia, Indonesia remains our largest overseas market. Offline Buy Now, Pay Later accounted for approximately 31% of volume this quarter, up from approximately 25% in prior quarter and 10% a year ago. The shift is deliberate.

Offline Buy Now, Pay Later is originated at point of sales, so the use of funds is easier to verify and the customer relationship runs through a merchant network rather than a paid traffic. With acquisition costs and regulatory scrutiny both rising, that is a more durable way to grow. In the Philippines, volume edged down 5% sequentially as the business cycled under the new rate cap. Credit performance came in better than expected, helped by another typhoon season and the conservative underwriting we put in place over the past 3 quarters. We believe the transition to the lower rate cap is largely complete, and we look to restart growth in the coming quarters. In Australia, volume grew 21% sequentially.

The growth came with a better customer mix with higher quality middle to large loans rising to around half of the portfolio. The key was the deeper integration with Australia's open banking regime, which gave us verified transaction level data on income, spending, and existing debt in place of self-declared inputs. That data works in 2 directions. We can approach good credit applicants we would previously have declined, and we can price and size credit limits more accurately for everyone else. As a result, conversion among good credit applicants improved while our underwriting standards held firm. On the foundation, we are now adding larger ticket products for borrowers with stronger credit profiles, and we expect the mix shift to continue.

Technology remains to -- how we run this business and this quarter, it mattered more than ever. As the collection campaign cut industry capacity, our AI collection agents helped offset part of the lost external capacity. Two of our AI applicants, including our customer service agent built on our own large language model, [ RAG Fin ] were also named exemplary cases in Shanghai's 2026 Collection Of Outstanding Artificial Intelligence Applicants. The agents has served more than 100 million users with a roughly 7% relative increase in self-service resolution. To close, at the start of this year, we said we would move through 2026 with patience and caution.

China's reset is still underway, and we expect the industry to keep evolving around risk, funding, and regulation. We expect to come through it with a clearly more resilient China business and overseas business that contributes a growing share of our profit. We entered this period with RMB 8.4 billion in cash and short-term investments and a strong diversified overseas business that continues to grow. I will now turn the call over to our CFO, Alexis, for a deeper look at the numbers.

Jiayuan Xu: Thank you, Tim, and hello, everyone. Let me go through our key results for the third quarter. Please refer to our earnings press release for further details. I will cover each segment and then the group results. First, China. Our priorities this quarter were funding stability, risk mitigation, and cost discipline. China revenue was RMB 1.5 billion, down 38% sequentially, reflecting a smaller loan book, partially cushioned by deferred revenue from legacy loan cohorts. Take rate for this quarter landed at around 2.9%, down 30 basis points from the second quarter due to rising credit and funding costs.

Our credit risk indicators remain elevated for 2 reasons: The industry-wide funding squeeze and the collection campaign that began at the end of July. For loans originated in the third quarter, we expect vintage cost of 3.5% compared with 2.7% for the second quarter. Early risk indicators showed a smaller -- a similar trend. Network delinquency rose from 5.3% to 6.4%, mainly from legacy loans originated before funding tightened. The 30-day collection rate fell from 89% to 85% as collection capacity across the industry strength. As a result, CM2 rose from 0.56% to 0.92%.

As Tim mentioned, we are tightening risk controls on 3 fronts: be selective with new customers, lend to the people we know best, and shut out fraud. First, our new customers with funding tight across the industry, risk surface faster and the quality of the new customer traffic deteriorated. So we became much more selective. We tracked how stress were spread across the industry and used those signals to adjust our approval strategy and the credit limit models early. We shifted our channel mix toward higher quality source and cut off high-risk traffic. Second, our existing customers in a tougher environment, we focus on funding borrowers we know well.

But even familiar borrowers can change quickly when credit drives up elsewhere. So we looked more closely at 3 warning signs: Borrowing from multiple plans, pipe platforms, high debt relative to income, and unstable repayment. We re-evaluated the portfolio by risk-adjusted return, reduced exposure to borrowers showing these signs, and moved the credit limits to those with proven track records. We also added specific controls for borrowers who repeatedly paid a few days later and early signs of stress. This keeps our risk exposure contained. Put credit in steady hands and ease pressure on collections. Third on fraud. When credit tightens, fraud becomes more organized. First through intermediaries and the connected networks that one-by-one application checks cannot catch.

We strengthened pattern detection across money flows, intermediary behavior, application networks, and the devices. We will remain vigilant as we entered the fourth quarter and continue to prioritize loan quality. During the quarter, funding costs also rose from 3.7% to about 4.3% as the tight supply pushed up the cost of funding from a low base. We work closely with our funding partners to restore supply, while funding has not yet returned to normal level. It has recovered to a level sufficient for our current risk appetite and origination plan. Moving on to overseas. Operating profit was RMB 62 million, up 15% sequentially and more than double the prior year level.

Demand remained resilient, and we added 2.8 million new borrowers, up 28% sequentially. That said, the macro backdrop across our markets has become more challenging. Higher oil prices are driving inflation and monetary policy is tightening across the region. We are managing each market with that environment. In Indonesia, the rupiah has become under sustained pressure and the Bank Indonesia has raised its policy rate by 25 basis points to year high 6% to support the currency. Consumer confidence has softened with the index falling from 127 in July -- in January to 120 in October. We are monitoring credit quality closely and are ready to adjust our risk strategy early if conditions warrant.

In the Philippines, inflation rose to 7.2% in September and the Central Bank has raised 4x in a row, most recently to 5.5%. As they scaled back origination ahead of this, we are well-positioned to restart growth at a measured pace. In Australia, the Reserve Bank has raised its cash rate 5x this year to 4.85%, and though we are mindful of the pressure, this puts out household budgets. Even so, demand for digital credit adoption remains strong, and we expect Australia to keep contributing to both growth and profit. Overseas operating profit for the first 9 months totaled about RMB 161 million, and we remain on track for our full year target of about USD 30 million in EBITDA.

And at the group level, net revenue was RMB 2.6 billion, down 25% sequentially. Operating expenses were RMB 1,048 million, down 27% sequentially, mainly due to lower sales and marketing spend as we serve more repeat customers through our own app rather than paid traffic. Total provision expense was broadly flat at RMB 1.35 billion despite lower origination, reflecting higher credit costs on legacy loans. Net profit was RMB 206 million, down 52%. Our balance sheet remains liquid and strong. Cash and short-term investments totaled RMB 8.4 billion. Our leverage ratio, defined as risk-bearing loan balance divided by shareholders' equity was 1.5x, a historical low. Our shareholder returns -- our capital allocation policy is unchanged.

Our current priority is to meet the operational needs of our business while optimizing our cost of debt. We repurchased USD 4.3 million of shares in the third quarter, bringing repurchase for the first 9 months to USD 71.1 million. In October, we prioritized capital toward our convertible senior notes, repurchasing about USD 19 million, which reduces our debt and the potential dilution. We remain committed to returning capital to shareholders based on sustainable normalized earnings and cash flow. Now to our outlook. Despite the third quarter's disruptions, group revenue reached RMB 9.2 billion for the first 3 quarters.

We are, therefore, reiterating our full year revenue guidance of RMB 11.5 billion to RMB 12.9 billion and expect to land at the lower end of that range. This reflects our current and preliminary views subject to any changes to the operating environment. To sum up, the third quarter showed both the cost of China's reset and the value of the choices we made before and during it. We cut volume early, tightened risk, and amended our funding relationships. We believe that the new environment will favor players with strong compliance discipline and operational know-how. Meanwhile, our overseas business continued to grow both its top and bottom lines.

We are clear-eyed about the domestic challenges ahead and firmly committed to disciplined execution through the coming quarters with our focus on long-term returns for our shareholders. That concludes my prepared remarks. We will now open the call to the questions. Operator, please continue.

Our 30-day collection rate fell from 89.4% to 85.5% and our CM2 flow rate rose from 0.56% to 0.92%. Our vintage estimate for loans originated in Q3 is 3.5%, up 80 basis points from the Q2 vintage. In terms of how we have responded, we started to tighten our risk controls in July and reduced our exposure further in August and September, shifting our focus from volume growth to asset quality. For those accounts, the monthly recovery rate with AI is almost 1.5x the rate third-party collection agencies achieved through phone calls. That shows how AI can help improve collections efficiency. #2854. Galicia (GGAL) Q3 2026 Earnings Call Transcript

Margins narrowed as Argentina's inflation cooled, and 2027 lending plans are taking shape.

By Motley Fool Transcribing – Nov 26, 2026 at 1:08AM EST

Need a quote from a Motley Fool analyst? Email [email protected]

Grupo Financiero Galicia (GGAL +3.90%) reported third-quarter results marked by a sequential compression in margins as the Argentine economy transitions toward lower inflation and reduced nominal interest rates. Management reported that while headline profitability fell 16% from the second quarter, underlying operational metrics improved through reduced loan loss provisions and lower administrative expenses. The company stated that the integration of HSBC Argentina is contributing to significant efficiency gains, with group expenses falling 36% year over year. The group maintained high liquidity and a strong capital position, which management stated provides strategic optionality to accelerate growth as credit demand recovers.

Operator: Good morning, ladies and gentlemen. Welcome to Grupo Financiero Galicia Third Quarter 2026 Earnings Call. This conference is being recorded, and the replay will be available at the company's website at gfgsa.com. [Operator Instructions] Some of the statements made during this conference call will be forward-looking statements within the meaning of the safe harbor provisions of the U.S. federal securities laws and are subject to risks and uncertainties that could cause actual results to differ materially from those expressed. Investors should be aware of events related to the macroeconomic scenario, the financial industry and other factors could cause results to differ materially from those expressed in the respective forward-looking statements. Now I will turn the conference over to Mr.

Pablo Eduardo Firvida, Head of Investor Relations. You may begin your conference.

Pablo Eduardo Firvida: Thank you. Good morning, everyone, and thank you for joining Grupo Galicia's Third Quarter 2026 Earnings Call. I'm here with Gonzalo Fernández Covaro, our Chief Financial Officer. The third quarter was marked by lower inflation and lower interest rates. Therefore, returns on inflation-linked assets declined and the credit cycle showed signs of improvement. For Grupo Galicia, this produced a quarter with lower sequential profitability, but also with encouraging developments beneath the headline result. Credit indicators improved, provisions declined, expenses continue to fall, and our liquidity and capital position remained very strong. In our presentation today, we will first describe the operating environment and then the results of the group and its subsidiaries.

Unless otherwise indicated, all figures are stated in constant Argentine pesos as of September 30, 2026. The macroeconomic environment continued to combine fiscal discipline with softer economic activity. According to the Monthly Indicator of Economic Activity, MIEA, (sic) [ Monthly Estimator of Economic Activity, EMAE, ] the Argentine economy contracted 1.2% year-over-year in September, while edging up 1.1% month-over-month on a seasonally adjusted basis. Activity levels stood 2.4% below those recorded in December 2025. As of September 2026, the primary fiscal surplus reached 1.2% of GDP with total revenues declining 4.3% year-over-year in real terms and primary spending falling 2.3%. At the same time, inflation continued to moderate.

Consumer prices increased 5.8% during the quarter compared with 6.8% in the second quarter. This was positive for purchasing power, planning and the long-term development of financial intermediation. In the short term, however, it also accelerated the decline in nominal interest rates and reduced the returns generated by CPI-linked assets. During the last 12 months, inflation reached 33.4% in September. The monetary base increased 0.9% in real terms during the third quarter and declined 16.4% in real terms compared with September 2025. The average exchange rate of the quarter was ARS 1,500 per dollar. And at the end of the quarter, the exchange rate reached ARS 1,517 per dollar, representing a 7.6% year-over-year depreciation.

Dollar deposits remained dynamic, while in pesos, we see the transactional deposits declined year-over-year and time deposits gained share during the period. In terms of credit, the system data reflected the transition process of the macro. Real peso credit remained soft, while dollar lending continued to expand at a faster pace. In other words, the financial system moved toward a lower inflation environment, but the volume recovery was still uneven, with the volume of dollar intermediation becoming more relevant. Taken together, these trends frame our quarter. Lower inflation reduced the loss from the net monetary position, but asset yields declined faster than funding costs and volume growth is still lagging.

As a result, margins compressed before loan demand and transactional activity had fully recovered. Against this backdrop, Grupo Galicia generated net income of ARS 229 billion in the third quarter with an annualized ROE of 9.3% and an ROA of 1.7%. The result was 16% below the previous quarter, but represented a significant recovery from the loss recorded 1 year earlier. More importantly, profitability remained diversified. Banco Galicia contributed with ARS 134.9 billion, Galicia Asset Management with ARS 48.5 billion, Naranja X with ARS 29.3 billion, Galicia Seguros with ARS 11.4 billion and Galicia Securities with ARS 5.3 billion. This diversification is strategically relevant as it helps to stabilize the group's earnings capacity.

For the first 9 months of 2026, net income reached ARS 577.5 billion with an 8% ROE. The performance shows that the group has moved beyond the losses recorded in 2025 and is rebuilding profitability on a more sustainable operating base. The decline in profitability from the second quarter was largely explained by the reduction in the group's financial margin, which fell to 14.4% as lower lending rates, lower CPI returns and fewer gains from security sales affected revenues. But this should be read together with positive developments. The cost of risk kept on decreasing from the peak recorded in December last year and expenses declined materially year-over-year, improving the efficiency ratio more than 20 percentage points.

Banco Galicia, our largest subsidiary, is where the change in the earnings environment was most visible. Net income reached ARS 134.9 billion with a 7% ROE. The 19% sequential decline in profitability was primarily a consequence of margin compression, partially offset by the improvement in most of the lines related to expenses, in which our focus in efficiencies is showing results. During the quarter, average interest-earning assets increased 2% to ARS 32 trillion, but their average yield declined to 22.3% from 27.1%. Average interest-bearing liabilities increased 1%, while their cost fell to 9% from 10.1%.

Funding costs moved in the right direction, but not as quickly as asset yields, which explains a significant part of the reduction in net interest income. Within the asset mix by currency, average peso loans grew 3%, foreign currency loans increased 9% and peso government securities declined 6%. This was a quarter of selective allocation with the bank reducing public sector exposure and prioritizing lending opportunities with an appropriate risk-adjusted return. Going to our interest-bearing liabilities, in the chart on the right, we can see the volume of those denominated in foreign currency, which increased by 6%. And on the left, that in pesos, they decreased 4%.

Interest income reached ARS 1.7 trillion, declining 12% sequentially and 24% year-over-year as lending rates and CPI returns fell. Interest expenses reached ARS 586 billion, also decreasing quarter-over-quarter. When we compare the figures with the ones of the third quarter of the previous year, the reduction was significant, mainly due to the increase in the cost of funding associated to the volatility of the midterm elections that took place last year. Fee income amounted to ARS 317 billion and decreased 3% in real terms in the third quarter, with most of the sources of fees behaving similarly, while results from financial instruments returned to more normalized levels after the strong gains recorded in the second quarter.

FX results amounted to ARS 119 billion and increased 3% in the quarter, mainly due to the trading activity with our clients. The most encouraging development of the quarter was the change in credit trends. Loan loss provisions declined 12% sequentially to ARS 509 billion. The NPL ratio improved to 7.8% from 8.3%. Cost of risk fell to 8% from 9.3% and coverage increased to 96.1% from 92.8%. Fewer exposures migrated to Stage 3, deterioration in the retail portfolio slowed and nonaccrual financing declined. This suggests that the credit cycle began to stabilize during the quarter. We remain cautious as NPL levels are still above those recorded 1 year ago and improvement must be sustained over several quarters.

Nevertheless, the direction of the trend is now positive. The actions taken in underwriting, collections, portfolio management and provisioning are beginning to translate into better asset quality metrics. This is important for the earnings outlook. As the cost of risk normalizes, a larger portion of operating income can translate into bottom line profitability even in a lower rate environment. The second major structural message is efficiency. Banco Galicia's expenses declined 10% quarter-over-quarter and 36% year-over-year to ARS 731 billion. Personnel expenses fell 15% sequentially. Administrative expenses declined 5% and other operating expenses were 10% lower. These reductions reflect the efficiency initiatives implemented throughout the year and the benefits of the integration with HSBC Argentina.

Lower maintenance, IT, communications and administrative service costs demonstrate that the combined platform is beginning to operate with greater scale. The group's efficiency ratio was 39.4%. It increased sequentially because revenues compressed faster than expenses, but it improved by more than 20 percentage points compared with the third quarter of the previous year. Our objective is to continue simplifying the operating model while protecting customer experience, digital capabilities and the capacity to grow. Financing to the private sector totaled ARS 26 trillion and declined 3% sequentially, reflecting the softer level of demand and our selective approach to origination. Market share of financing to the private sector decreased 30 basis points to 14.7%.

Deposits were stable at ARS 28.7 trillion, while private sector deposits market share increased 20 basis points to 14.5%. Peso-denominated deposits decreased 3%, while dollar deposits increased 3%. Time deposits in pesos increased 12%, while transactional deposits in pesos decreased 11%, negatively affecting the cost of funding. Liquidity remained high. Liquid assets represented 99.7% of transactional deposits and 56.1% of total deposits. Capitalization was equally strong with a total capital ratio of 25.7% and Tier 1 ratio of 25.6%. Computable capital exceeded the regulatory minimum by ARS 4.8 trillion. This buffer provides strategic optionality. They allow us to absorb volatility, protect the franchise and accelerate growth when demand returns and risk-adjusted spreads become more attractive.

The quarter also demonstrated the value of Grupo Galicia diversified ecosystem. While Banco Galicia managed the pressure from lower rates, the other companies contributed with other sources of earnings and customer engagement. Together, these businesses expand the relationship with our customers beyond traditional banking. They allow the group to participate in payments, consumer finance, insurance, savings and capital markets, creating a broader and more balanced platform for growth. Naranja X generated net income of ARS 29 billion. Its profitability declined sequentially to an ROE of 9.3%, but credit indicators improved. The NPL ratio fell to 17.8% and cost of risk to 17.9%.

The business continues to combine scale, digital engagement and financial inclusion while recalibrating growth to the current risk environment. Galicia Seguros reported net income of ARS 12 billion and a combined ratio of 92.8%, demonstrating the contribution of insurance to the group's fee and risk diversification strategy. Galicia Asset Management delivered net income of ARS 48 billion, while assets under management reached ARS 16.6 trillion. In a lower rate environment, the demand for savings and investment solution becomes increasingly important, and our asset management franchise is well positioned to capture that opportunity. Now Gonzalo Fernández Covaro, our CFO, will make some additional remarks.

Gonzalo Javier Fernández Covaro: Thank you, Pablo. Before taking your questions, I would like to add that the third quarter is part of a transition period. Lower inflation and lower rates are positive for Argentina and for the long-term development of financial intermediation, but they also changed the earnings equation faster than volumes could recover. This compressed margins and temporarily reduced profitability until volumes are recovered. At the same time, the underlying quality of our performance improved. Credit indicators began to turn, as we have said in calls -- in prior calls, provisions and cost of risk declined, expenses continue to fall and deposits remain stable and liquidity and capital stayed at very strong levels.

These developments gives us the confidence that the direction of the business is the correct one. We are moving from an environment in which earnings were heavily influenced by inflation, high nominal rates and financial assets towards one in which sustainable profitability will be driven increasingly by customer activity, transaction volumes, fees, disciplined credit growth and an efficient operating platform. Our priorities for the coming quarters are therefore clear. We will actively manage the balance sheet to protect margins and grow our earning assets. We will maintain discipline in origination and collections to consolidate improvement in asset quality.

We will continue capturing integration and efficiency benefits, and we will deploy capital selectively as attractive opportunities may emerge across our subsidiaries, Banco Galicia, Naranja X, Galicia Seguros and Galicia Asset Management. So Grupo Galicia enters next year with a leading franchise, a diversified ecosystem and substantial financial strength. The quarter showed that even as earnings mix changes, the foundations of the business are becoming stronger. So with that, we are ready to answer the questions that you may have.

But -- so for us, asset management is part of the banking business because it's the same customer, same everything.

We -- yes, the new ETF, I think it's a good thing for the market, but we don't expect that to -- we expect asset management to continue with a very good performance and the customers to continue to invest in our funds. So we don't see any impact on the contrary. So we expect asset management to -- and the funds to continue to be an important part of our income. Today, of course, as you know, the money market is the star. But as Argentina continue in a stabilization path, then other funds more sophisticated should start picking up.

So that's what we have been preparing for to have also other offers when the money market, the plain vanilla money market lose attractiveness, then what the other ones can bring. So we continue to expect to continue to keep and gain market share and continue to be one of the leading asset managers in the country and continue to have a good net income. And the other question talking about SMEs, small and medium enterprises, NPLs. I mean, it's true that NPLs for SMEs started after the consumer NPLs. For us, it's a small portfolio. So even though we saw an increase in NPLs, you don't see that in the numbers because it's -- the impact is not big.

We are starting to see some kind of stabilization, not reduction so far, stabilization. And by next year, we expect that those NPLs started to improve. But in the numbers of the bank, they are no material, so they don't move the needle in terms of NPLs as a whole.

Operator: We have another question from Ernesto Gabilondo from Bank of America.

Ernesto María Gabilondo Márquez: I have a question on competition. Mercado Pago seems to become a more relevant player in Argentina, especially after it obtains the banking license. So just wanted to hear from you what are the risks or concerns that you see once Mercado Pago starts operations?

Gonzalo Javier Fernández Covaro: I mean we take Mercado Pago very seriously as an important competitor before getting banking licenses and that will be after that. Of course, that will be -- I mean, on one hand, I think it's good because we are going to be all with the same regulations and the same taxes and everything similar. So that helps to put the playing field even. But of course, they will be able to do things that they cannot do now. So we take them serious as well we are implementing a lot of new value proposition.

We are -- we think that our right to win is also in the middle and middle upper segments that demands more sophisticated products and it's where we are also targeting new offers for customers around Éminent. And where we see that, again, we have more the right to win than a fintech because of the complexity of the products we can offer. We are also redesigning all our investment platform for next year for customers to give more value added on top of what a digital wallet can offer. With time, they will catch up for sure, and will be another competitor, and we are happy to compete.

But we believe that, again, we have high-level technology in terms of our app. We have also an Naranja X that competes with Mercado Pago directly with same products. And that's the way we have been competing and will continue. We expect also that the Argentina financial system to continue to grow. And as today, I think we are at 12% of loan to GDP that continue to grow, that also will give room for everyone to compete and to grow.

Operator: The question-and-answer session is over. We would like to hand the floor back to Pablo Eduardo Firvida for the company's final remarks.

Pablo Eduardo Firvida: Okay. Thank you all for joining this call. And well, I hope you have a great day and Happy Thanksgiving for the ones in the Northern Hemisphere. Bye-bye.

#2852. Robinhood shares fall as crypto slump weighs on profits, offsetting gains in new businesses

Robinhood shares were down around 8% in pre-market trading on Wednesday as investors digested weaker-than-expected third-quarter earnings despite growth in options trading and prediction markets.

Here's how the brokerage performed in its quarter ended Sept. 30, compared with Wall Street estimates, based on a survey of analysts by LSEG:

Robinhood's net income fell to $468 million, or 51 cents per share, from $556 million, or 61 cents per share, a year ago. Revenue represented a 12% increase from $1.27 billion in the same quarter last year.

Despite that gain, transaction-based revenue of $867 million came in below Wall Street estimates from StreetAccount of $879 million, putting focus on whether Robinhood can sustain momentum across its trading businesses and reduce its reliance on volatile market activity.

Robinhood has been diversifying its business as it tries to expand its brokerage app into a broader financial services platform. Options trading and event contracts have become increasingly important sources of transaction revenue, while the company has continued to invest in wealth management, subscriptions and other offerings.

Transaction-based revenue grew 19% from the same period last year to $867 million, driven primarily by a more than 500% increase in event contracts revenue. Additionally, options revenue grew 31% to $399 million and equities revenue rose 41% to $121 million.

Those gains were partially offset by cryptocurrency revenue, which fell 55% to $120 million.

Chief Financial Officer Shiv Verma told CNBC event contracts on crypto movements did well in the quarter, offering investors another way to express a view on crypto despite the slump in prices and spot trading.

Net deposits on the Robinhood platform totaled $15.7 billion for the quarter, equal to a 17% annualized growth rate based on total platform assets at the end of Q2 2026. Over the past year, net deposits totaled $71 billion, representing 21% growth compared with total platform assets at the end of Q3 2025.

—With reporting from CNBC's Robert Hum and Kate Rooney

#2849. Genpact: 3 Key Takeaways for Long-Term Investors

Genpact is accepting a two-point growth drag by dropping legacy accounts, a trade-off that tests how patient long-term shareholders must be.

By The Motley Fool Team – Nov 26, 2026 at 1:43AM EST

On its third-quarter 2026 earnings call, held on Nov. 5, 2026, Genpact (G +3.12%) announced that full-year revenue growth will reach at least 7% as its automated artificial intelligence strategy, called Agentic Operations, takes hold. For long-term investors, the corporate pivot shifts the business away from labor-heavy services toward recurring software-like revenue streams.

Structural margin gains and rapid technology adoption position the enterprise for compounding long-term returns. Three things management said on the call stand out.

The company is accelerating its transition into Agentic Operations, expanding its total addressable market (TAM) -- the total revenue opportunity available -- and locking in higher-margin recurring contracts.

By shifting client contracts away from full-time equivalent, or FTE, labor billing toward outcome-based software arrangements, gross margin expanded to 36.9%.

Genpact is deliberately dropping legacy accounts that do not align with its technology strategy, creating a short-term two-percentage-point growth drag to secure long-term profitability.

Demand for automated enterprise workflows is expanding, lifting gross margin and building long-term contract backlog. Deep process intelligence and proprietary software assets provide structural advantages over legacy outsourcing peers. Management is watching execution speed and client rotation into recurring digital agreements.

Long-term performance depends on converting contract wins into durable recurring revenue while capturing market share across major global clients.

#2848. Kevin Porter Jr. reportedly to sign two-year deal with Orlando Magic

Orlando has been looking for some scoring punch at the guard spot and has turned to free agent Kevin Porter Jr., who has agreed to sign a two-year deal with the team, his agents told ESPN's Shams Charania.

While the terms of the deal were not announced, this is undoubtedly for the veteran minimum, and the second year is very likely non-guaranteed.

Orlando was one of two teams reportedly interested in Porter earlier in the week. The other was Denver, but it didn't want to make a move right now.

While the Magic play Jalen Suggs and Anthony Black for most of the guard minutes, they want some scoring off the bench, and Porter can give them that. Last season in 38 games with the Bucks he averaged 17.4 points, 7.4 assists and 5.2 rebounds per game. He can be a streaky shooter and is not much of a defender, but in a backup role this can work.

Orlando had an open roster spot, so no other moves are needed. However, they are already over the luxury tax line, meaning this veteran minimum contract will cost them closer to $10 million total.

#2841. The Celtics taught the Wizards’ defense a lesson

During the Washington Wizards' much-needed three-game win streak, they made a few key adjustments. They yanked underperformers out of the rotation and limited the Deandre Ayton solo-big minutes, in which the Wizards have gotten caved in all season. They played Trae Young off the ball more and gave Kyshawn George and AJ Dybantsa more creation responsibility.

More than anything, though, they cleaned up some of the low-hanging fruit. They grabbed more rebounds, matched up better in transition, and eased some of the most damaging defensive mistakes. They knocked down some more open threes. The Celtics, in Tuesday night's primetime (first half) onslaught, showed the Wizards how far they still have to go.

Boston is a team built to punish glaring weaknesses. They relentlessly attack bad defenders, and of course were merciless in dragging Young into every action and forcing the Wizards to make difficult rotations. The Celtics scored 80 first half points and poured in 15 threes — the sort of Mazulla-ball explosion that they've been waiting to unleash.

Too often, the Wizards were slow to react to the Celtics' core actions. Boston, when they're locked in, is adept at running early-clock plays that prey on inattentive defenses, like this flare screen:

In semi-transition, the Wizards are cross-matched, a common hazard of the double-big alignment. The Celtics are on the same page: Neemias Queta steps into a screen for a trailing Jayson Tatum on an unsuspecting Dybantsa.

Dybantsa realizes slightly too late that he needs to switch the screen and take Queta. By then, it's too late. The floor is open, and Queta has cleverly slipped to the basket.

(Note, too, that Sarr and Davis are the weak side defenders here. In a few situations on Tuesday night, the Celtics generated open threes by capitalizing on that particular alignment. Sarr and Davis are slower at completing the "X-out" rotation, with the low man swarming the rim and the other playing two shooters at once.)

Here's another classic Celtics action, which Kevin analyzed in his recap:

Again, the Celtics get right into their action. Derrick White is set up for a Spain, or stack, pick-and-roll — where he sets a back-screen on a dropping big man. White is still so valuable to the Celtics, despite his ice-cold shooting, in part because of plays like this; he is clever about screening his own man and disguising his intentions.

On this one, White's screen slams Ayton and springs Paul George into the paint. The problem there starts with Dybantsa, who is hit hard by Luka Garza's initial screen and forced into a switch. To his credit, Tre Johnson sees what's happening and recovers toward George. But Bilal Coulibaly senses disaster and sprints over to help, leaving Payton Pritchard alone.

It's these emergency defense instincts that the Wizards struggle with. Coulibaly is right that he may be needed at the rim, but he commits so fully that he has no chance to rotate back to Pritchard. The most in-sync defenses are on a string when the offense gains an advantage — perhaps, in this situation, with the guy in Dybantsa's spot peeling off to the corner.

The Wizards have recently been better at erring on the side of effort defensively, and that's the first step toward building something real. Dybantsa has room to grow on the little things, like screen navigation, but he flies around and pressures relentlessly. Coulibaly knows he's on the court for his defense and plays like it.

When opposing teams line up transition layups and grab endless offensive rebounds, that's when the pressure on coach Brian Keefe starts heating up. But lately, that pressure is getting a bit lighter, and the second half against the Celtics was much better, with Boston cooling off and the Wizards limiting some of the easiest baskets.

They'll have a few days to rest and take stock before a rematch with the Orlando Magic on Saturday.

#2838. Vikings-Falcons First Injury Report

The Minnesota Vikings and Atlanta Falcons released their first injury reports in advance of their matchup on Sunday at U.S. Bank Stadium. Here they are:

PUP = Physically unable to participate list.

IR = Injured reserve. Note: only starters on IR are listed.

Probable = the league defines a Probable injury designation as a 75% or greater chance of playing. Historically, 95% of players designated probable are active and played on game day.

Questionable = the league defines a Questionable injury designation as being 'uncertain' of whether the player will play or not. This replaces the former definition of having a 50% chance of playing. Historically, 70-75% of players with a Questionable injury designation were active and played on game day.

Doubtful = The league defines a Doubtful injury designation to mean the player is unlikely to participate in the game. In practical terms, teams use the designation when a player has less than a 50% chance to suit up, and active game appearances from this designation remain rare. Historically only around 6% of players with a Doubtful designation are active and played on game day.

Out = will not play. Players with this designation are not active on game day and league rules prohibit them from playing.

For the Vikings, they held a walk-through on Wednesday with several starters listed as DNP, which doesn't bode well for Sunday. Hopefully some/most of these will be upgraded to limited or better tomorrow and Friday. I doubt Jordan Addison will be active on Sunday, however. And when Christian Darrisaw is a DNP with an injury, his track record is he doesn't play that week. We'll see.

Kyler Murray has also been upgraded to a limited participant, so he could end up being the primary backup to Kirk Cousins at some point.

For the Falcons, the more significant names on the list are starting guard Matthew Bergeron and starting wide receiver Jahan Dotson. Neither practiced on Wednesday so something to keep an eye on the rest of the week.

We'll have updates tomorrow and Friday with injury designations.

#2836. Top Wall Street analysts are bullish on these 3 stocks for the long haul

The ongoing earnings season, Middle East tensions and buzz ahead of the U.S. midterm elections have been keeping investors busy. Further, worries about elevated artificial intelligence spending are weighing on investor sentiment.

Investors looking beyond the short-term noise and focusing on long-term returns can track top Wall Street analysts to pick the right stocks, backed by strong research.

Here are three stocks favored by some of Wall Street's top pros, according to TipRanks, a platform that ranks analysts based on their past performance.

This week's first stock pick is Datadog, an AI-powered observability and security platform. The company will announce its results for the third quarter on Nov. 4. Datadog is seeing strong demand for its offerings, thanks to cloud migration and rapid AI adoption. TipRanks' KPIs tool for DDOG shows the growth in its customer base over the past few years amid increasing market penetration and growing adoption of its products.

Heading into Q3 FY26 earnings, Bank of America analyst Koji Ikeda reiterated a buy rating on Datadog stock and raised his price target to $335 from $305. The analyst noted that industry checks and data indicate that infrastructure software demand is strengthening, with AI increasingly driving usage and growth.

Ikeda called Datadog and Atlassian his top picks, saying that companies that can show that AI is a tailwind to growth could see their valuation rerate, even at premium multiples. He added that the total addressable market for observability is growing faster than anticipated, driven by accelerated growth of enterprise AI and the rapid ramp of consumer AI agents.

The five-star analyst believes that focus will be on revenue growth, which he estimates to be 28.7% for Q3 FY26 versus the Street's consensus of 29.1%. Ikeda thinks that the market is underestimating the growth prospects of DDOG's security products. Meanwhile, he cautioned investors about concentration risk related to large customers.

"We think results will be supportive of our long-term thesis that Datadog is a strong share gainer in the rapidly expanding observability opportunity," said Ikeda.

Ikeda ranks No. 673 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been successful 55% of the time, delivering an average return of 10.9%. See Datadog Statistics on TipRanks.

The next pick, semiconductor giant Nvidia, is also benefiting from AI-driven demand for its products. After attending Nvidia's GTC Berlin conference, Morgan Stanley analyst Joseph Moore reaffirmed a buy rating on NVDA stock with a price target of $300, calling it a top pick in the semiconductor space.

The five-star analyst said that the conference supported his thesis that Nvidia continues to effectively navigate bottlenecks in the supply chain, land/power/shell, and funding. In fact, Moore is "convinced that all 3 aspects are opportunities for NVDA."

While Nvidia's GTC Berlin offered no major updates, Moore noted the conference reflected that the company continues to add to its significant advantages heading into 2027. The analyst said that Vera Rubin is gaining momentum, though details on next year's Rubin Ultra were lighter than expected.

With demand outpacing supply, Moore said that focus is on closing the gap. In this regard, he highlighted the supply chain deals on substrate, packaging and testing. While the analyst continues to view TSMC 3 nm wafer supply as the biggest challenge, he expects it to ease over time.

Moore ranks No. 142 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 59% of the time, delivering an average return of 23.4%. See Nvidia Ownership Structure on TipRanks.

Aerospace company SpaceX recently announced solid third-quarter results, with its AI revenue surging to $7.56 billion from $813 million in the prior-year quarter.

Impressed by the results, Needham analyst Ryan Koontz reiterated a buy rating on SPCX stock with a price target of $250. Koontz highlighted that SpaceX's Q3 results significantly exceeded expectations, proving that the company's vertically integrated business model is driving massive scale.

Furthermore, Koontz emphasized the 195.2% sequential growth in SpaceX's AI revenue, fueled by wholesale spot compute deals. He noted management's increasing focus on its AI strategy, given that it is optimistic about adding 10 gigawatts of incremental capacity during 2027.

"With increasing confidence on Starship re-use, we meaningfully increase our estimates," said Koontz.

#2835. RJ Luis suing SEC and Greg Sankey after LSU basketball eligibility fight

The saga that's engulfed LSU, the Tigers' men's basketball program and the SEC has taken yet another turn.

And, to the surprise of no one, it involves lawyers.

RJ Luis, the one-time LSU men's basketball player whose eligibility case has been a point of contention for weeks with the SEC, has filed a lawsuit against the SEC and commissioner Greg Sankey seeking damages caused by the conference's efforts that prompted LSU's decision not to play him following a previously awarded court injunction, acccording to a motion and other documents provided by one of Luis' attorneys to The Daily Advertiser, a part of the USA TODAY Network.

REQUIRED READING: LSU played chicken with the SEC — and finally blinked. Was it worth it? | Opinion

It's unclear how much Luis is seeking in damages.

Luis, who had previously signed NBA contracts with the Utah Jazz and Boston Celtics, was granted two separate injunctions in Louisiana courts, the first on Aug. 3 and another Nov. 13, as he sought eligibility for the 2026-27 college basketball season. Luis committed to play for Tigers coach Will Wade in May.

While Luis was cleared to play by the judicial system, he faced an additional obstacle with the SEC, which in August passed a rule barring member institutions from having athletes compete for them who had previously declared for the NFL, NBA or WNBA drafts.

Following the Nov. 13 injunction, the SEC issued a statement saying it would abide by the court's order and not punish LSU for putting Luis on its roster, but the league mentioned nothing about the Tigers actually playing Luis. On Nov. 18, however, Luis played 15 minutes, scoring 11 points and pulling down nine rebounds, in his team's 79-67 victory against Louisiana Monroe.

One day later, LSU president Wade Rousseau released a statement saying he instructed Wade not to play Luis, who has since been removed from the LSU roster and hasn't played in the team's two most recent games.

That didn't mark the end of the escalating tensions between LSU and the league of which it's a founding member. The SEC's chancellors and presidents are scheduled to hold a vote on Dec. 3 over whether to expel the Tigers from the conference. A two-thirds vote from the 15 non-LSU members is required for the action to pass.

A 6-foot-7 guard/forward, Luis averaged 18.2 points and 7.2 rebounds per game for St. John's during the 2024-25 season, which culminated with him being named Big East player of the year and earning All-American honors. He declared for the 2025 NBA Draft, but was not selected by a team.

#2831. What we learned as De'Anthony Melton's sharp shot fuels Warriors' win vs. Kings

What we learned as De'Anthony Melton's sharp shot fuels Warriors' win vs. Kings originally appeared on NBC Sports Bay Area

SAN FRANCISCO – Thanksgiving dinner came early for the Warriors on Wednesday night as they feasted on the Sacramento Kings' defense early to enjoy a big enough lead that lasted throughout the game.

The Warriors scored just 88 points Sunday against the Boston Celtics. They then scored half of that in the first quarter against the Kings in an eventual 129-110 win at Chase Center.

After leading the Kings 44-19 through one quarter, the Warriors lost the final three quarters 91-85.

Overall, the Warriors shot 53.3 percent from the field, made 20 threes and tallied 37 assists with just 15 turnovers.

De'Anthony Melton led the way with 24 points, tying his career high of eight 3-pointers in a game. Melton in the Warriors' previous six games had made a total of eight threes.

This was not a one-man show, though. The Warriors had seven players score in double figures, and they won the bench battle 61-19 behind Gui Santos' 22 points.

Here are three takeaways from the Warriors' second straight win.

They play different positions and have much different roles, but whenever Yaxel Lendeborg plays another top pick making a name for himself, eyes instantly will turn to a rookie showdown. On Wednesday night, that meant pinning him against Kings rookie point guard Darius Acuff Jr.

The start of the game wasn't pretty for Lendeborg. Within the first four minutes, Lendeborg already airballed a three, was called for traveling and an offensive foul. He was replaced by Santos after the offensive foul, who instantly drained a 3-pointer.

However, Lendeborg did have three steals in the first quarter and was up to 11 points at halftime while Acuff was held to four.

Though Acuff (22 points) had more points than Lendeborg (15 points), the Warriors rookie had a much more efficient night. Lendeborg was a plus-8 thriving at the cup, as a cutter and screening for others, and Acuff was a minus-23, going a lowly 9 of 24 from the field and 3 of 12 beyond the arc.

The wait is over. Brandon Williams has been itching to make his return from a mild hamstring strain and immediately made an impact off the bench.

Williams watched the Warriors dominate the first quarter, and quickly took advantage of the Kings' lack of defense. On the first play of the second quarter, Williams used a ballscreen from Charles Bassey and a backscreen from Will Richard to blow by his defender and finish at the rim. He then went coast to coast, zooming past the Kings for another layup on the Warriors' next possession.

After a strong start, Williams missed his next four shots, failing to finish at the rim. A blocked shot on Keegan Murray got him going again in the third quarter, as Williams quickly converted on the other side.

Some rust had to be scraped off Williams throughout the night. His 14 points in 13 minutes and ability to attack the Kings' defense also played a big role in the Warriors' bench outscoring them by 42 points.

As the Warriors felt multiple times during their recent winless five-game road trip, this game was over in a hurry. It's a four-quarter game, but the doors were closed after the first quarter in favor of the Warriors. Melton catching fire from deep early was a big reason why.

His 3-point shooting was a huge plus to start the season as Melton had a 52.8 3-point percentage (19 of 36) through the first seven games of the season. Melton then went 0 of 9 over his next two games and has struggled to find his shot as of late. He missed all five of his 3-point attempts Sunday night against the Celtics, and then went 6 of 7 against the Kings in the first half, tying a season high for an entire game.

Melton made four of the Warriors' nine threes in the first quarter. The veteran combo guard then tacked on another two threes in the second quarter, and missed his only attempt in the third. The first time he fired from downtown in the fourth quarter might have been his most difficult attempt of the night, and it still swished through the net.

Scoring bursts always are an added bonus for Melton. Needing to find his way out of a funk, Melton's strong start propelled the Warriors to another home win.

Financial Market Utilities & Infrastructures

For release at 10:00 a.m. EDT Share

The Federal Reserve Board on Friday released the results of the 2025 Survey of Consumer Finances, which provides the public and policymakers with detailed insights into the economic condition of American families.

The data provide a representative picture of what Americans own, how and how much they borrow, and how they bank. The current version of the survey has been undertaken every three years since 1989 and provides the public with an extensive time series of thorough information on the economic conditions of American families. Alongside the survey data, the Board issued its summary report entitled Changes in U.S. Family Finances from 2022 to 2025 (PDF), which examines changes in income, net worth, assets, debt, and financial vulnerability.

Key findings from the 2025 survey include:

In addition to the summary report, an interactive chartbook displaying changes in these financial characteristics over time is available here. Additional data from the survey, including extensive data examining other economic conditions of American families, is made available to researchers and the public here.

The survey is conducted for the Board by NORC, a social science research organization at the University of Chicago. Participants in the study are chosen at random from 119 geographic areas, including metropolitan areas and rural counties across the United States, using scientific sampling procedures that aim to produce a sample representative of American families.

#2826. Lakers’ red-hot start to season will strike fear in Celtics fans hearts

The Lakers are on a quest to become a championship-level team.

And if history can be an indicator, their hot start to the 2026-27 NBA season suggests they're on the right path.

Lakers star guards Luka Doncic (77) and Austin Reaves (15). NBAE via Getty Images

With Monday's home win over the Jazz, the Lakers improved to 15-3 on the season, which was not only tied with the Spurs for the league's best record, but also a positive sign for the Lakers' hopes of being title contenders.

This season is the sixth time this century the Lakers have started a season by winning at least 15 of their first 18 games.

All five of those seasons ended with the Lakers making the NBA Finals, including winning the title in four of them.

Los Angeles Lakers head coach JJ Redick watches his players. AP Photo/Kyusung Gong

The other five times since 2000 that the Lakers won at least 83 percent of their first 18 games were: 2001-2002 (16-2), 2003-04 (15-3), 2008-09 (16-2), 2009-10 (15-3) and 2019-20 (16-2).

They won the 2002, '09, '10 and '20 championships after their hot start to those seasons.

They made it to the Finals in 2004, which was their fourth championship series appearance in five years, before losing to the Pistons in five games.

Walker Kessler, left, and Luka Doncic high-five during a game. NBAE via Getty Images

The Lakers have won at least 15 of their first 18 games 13 times in franchise history.

Half (six of 13) of those seasons ended with them winning the NBA title, while three more ended with them at least making it to the NBA Finals.

Another commonality between those seasons is that the Lakers won at least 54 regular season games (or the equivalent of it in an 82-game season in the case of the pandemic-shortened 2019-20) each time they had such a hot start to the season.

For a team that was projected to be a borderline playoff contender, that's great company to be in — a high standard to live up to — but also a sign they're on the right path.
