#2820. Federal Reserve issues FOMC statement

An official website of the United States Government

Official websites use .govA .gov website belongs to an official government organization in the United States.

Secure .gov websites use HTTPSA lock (LockLocked padlock icon) or https:// means you've safely connected to the .gov website. Share sensitive information only on official, secure websites.

The Federal Reserve, the central bank of the United States, provides the nation with a safe, flexible, and stable monetary and financial system.

Review of Monetary Policy Strategy, Tools, and Communications

Mergers, Acquisitions, and Other Applications

Financial Stability Coordination & Actions

Financial Market Utilities & Infrastructures

For release at 2:00 p.m. EDT Share

The Federal Open Market Committee approved the following statement for release by a 10 â 2 vote:

The Committee decided to maintain the target range for the federal funds rate at 3-3/4 to 4 percent, in support of the Federal Reserve's dual mandate. The Committee is continuing its policy of maintaining ample reserves in the banking system.

Economic activity is expanding at a strong pace. While uncertainty remains elevated owing, in part, to geopolitical developments, domestic spending has been resilient. Productivity growth is strong, and capital investment is robust. Job gains have kept pace with the workforce, and the unemployment rate has changed little.

Inflation remains elevated. The accumulation of more information will help the Committee determine the policy stance appropriate to ensure a timely return of inflation to the Committee's 2 percent goal. The Committee will deliver price stability.

Voting against the monetary policy action were Lisa D. Cook and Beth M. Hammack, who preferred to raise the target range for the federal funds rate by 1/4 percentage point at this meeting.

#2815. Austin Reaves returns to Lakers practice, might play Friday at San Antonio

After missing two weeks because of a bruised left heel, Lakers guard Austin Reaves returned to live play in practice Wednesday and could be on track to return in time for the Lakers' early season litmus test against the Spurs at San Antonio on Friday.

Lakers coach JJ Redick deadpanned that Reaves' practice performance Wednesday, the team's last session before traveling on Thanksgiving Day, was "the best I've ever seen him play." Reaves was more understated.

"It felt good to play basketball again," Reaves said. "Wind was a little bad, but kind of to be expected being out [for] two weeks. But felt good. We'll see how I wake up tomorrow. We're still going in the right direction, but felt really good today."

Reaves, the Lakers' second-leading scorer, missed five games in which the Lakers (15-3) went 4-1 and remained atop the NBA standings to set up Friday's marquee matchup between the top two teams in the league. He was injured on Nov. 11 during the third quarter against the Milwaukee Bucks when on a dunk attempt, twisting in the air and landing so hard on the inside edge of his heel that he noticed an indent on his shoe insole. He tried to keep playing, but a painful baseline drive forced him to ask out of the game.

"I didn't think I broke my foot," Reaves said Wednesday, "but it sure felt like I broke my foot."

While managing the pain during his return to play, Reaves has worked with the training staff to find a comfortable sneaker and insole combination. Reaves said through the seemingly million options presented to him, he found one that worked for Wednesday's practice.

Read more: Lakers eagerly await Austin Reaves' return before epic showdown against San Antonio

"He looked really good today," center Walker Kessler said. "It was definitely good to have him back out there."

After signing a maximum contract extension to establish himself as one of the Lakers' top stars, Reaves got off to a strong start to the season. He is averaging 24.7 points, 4.7 assists and 3.3 rebounds while shooting a career-best 51.3% from the field and 40.9% from three. The Lakers had won six consectutive games heading into the Milwaukee matchup when Reaves got hurt. Even when Reaves was sidelined, they stretched the streak to nine, tying the longest in Redick's coaching tenure.

"I was just frustrated because it's one of those things, it's like an uncontrollable," Reaves said. "... One of those things that just, it sucks. I was rolling, playing well. We were rolling as a team, wanted to continue to be out there and play games. Hate missing games. But it's one of those things, like I said, there wasn't much that we could really do, but we took care of business."

The Lakers' offense has stalled without Reaves, dropping from first in scoring and efficiency to third. Luka Doncic is especially struggling with his shot. The league's leading scorer, averaging 36.6 points per game, has made 12 of 51 (23.5%) shots from three during the last four games.

With Reaves out, Doncic got help from reserve guards Quentin Grimes and Collin Sexton. Grimes started in Reaves' place and delivered a game-saving, career-high 47 points against the Charlotte Hornets. Sexton served as a primary ballhandler during critical fourth-quarter stretches without Doncic.

"[We] found ways to win," Reaves said. "I think that's a big part of the NBA is if you can find ways to win when you're not playing as good as you are capable of, that'll help win in a lot of games. So very happy, proud just to continue to see the togetherness and the growth that we continue to have."

The Lakers stuck together to set up Friday's showdown that is stocked with intriguing storylines. The NBA Cup group stage finale is a win-and-you're-in matchup for the knockout round. Most valuable player candidates Doncic and Victor Wembanyama, averaging 29.9 points, 12.3 rebounds and 3.9 blocks a game, face off for the first time this season. Questioned for a perceived weaker schedule, the Lakers get their first major opportunity to assert themselves into the title conversation against the reigning Western Conference champions.

The Spurs (15-3) have the best net rating in the NBA, outscoring opponents by 12.3 points per 100 possessions. The Lakers, suddenly a top-10 defensive team after holding the Utah Jazz to only 94 points in Monday's win, are sixth in net rating (plus-6.8 points per 100 possessions).

Despite all the makings of an epic early-season showdown, Redick emphasized that Friday's blockbuster matchup is only the beginning of a difficult four-game road trip. After San Antonio, the Lakers play at Memphis on Sunday, Houston on Monday and New Orleans on Wednesday.

"I think these four games will be a great barometer of where we're at," Redick said. "To your point whether it's guys being in and out of the lineup or play-style changes or tweaks we make or rotation changes, it just always evolves.

"And whether we lose the game, whether we win the game, we're either going to be contenders on 'First Take' or we're not going to be contenders and we're fake. That stuff is just noise. We're going to focus on the process of trying to get better and it's a good test to see where we're at."

Sign up for our weekly newsletter on all things Lakers.

This story originally appeared in Los Angeles Times.

#2814. Top Wall Street analysts like these 3 dividend stocks for consistent income

Democrats' midterm election win has put the artificial intelligence trade in question, as voters clearly expressed opposition to the rapid buildout of data centers. The stock market could remain volatile amid AI trade concerns, macro uncertainties, and geopolitical tensions.

In this scenario, investors can add dividend-paying stocks to their portfolios to earn steady income. However, the extensive universe of dividend stocks can make selection challenging. Top Wall Street analysts' recommendations can help investors shortlist attractive dividend stocks, as these experts assign ratings after in-depth financial analysis.

Here are three dividend-paying stocks that are highlighted by Wall Street's top pros, as tracked by TipRanks, a platform that ranks analysts based on their past performance.

Viper Energy (VNOM) is a subsidiary of Diamondback and owns and acquires mineral and royalty interests, primarily in the Permian Basin. The company reported upbeat third-quarter earnings, supported by recent acquisitions and continued development activity from Diamondback and third-party operators. Viper announced a base cash dividend of 50 cents per share for Q3 2026, payable on Nov. 19. Based on the base and variable dividends announced over the past 12 months, VNOM's dividend yield stands at 5.7%.

Following the Q3 results, TD Cowen analyst Aaron Bilkoski reiterated a buy rating on Viper Energy stock with a price target of $61. The analyst noted that the company delivered solid Q3 results, with production, including oil and natural gas liquids, exceeding both TD Cowen and consensus estimates.

The 5-star analyst highlighted that the key update was the announcement of two royalty acquisitions for $809 million in cash, including deferred consideration, and $82 million of newly issued shares.

Given the recently announced base dividend hike, Bilkoski expects Viper to direct incremental free cash flow toward buybacks. While the acquisitions increase leverage, the analyst believes that Viper has enough capacity for opportunistic buybacks.

Bilkoski noted that Viper has historically delivered one of the highest production per share growth profiles in his royalty coverage, but still trades at a notable discount to the royalty peer average and the company's historical FCF yield range. "In our view, its above-average growth warrants a higher valuation," said the analyst.

Bilkoski ranks No. 525 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 57% of the time, delivering an average return of 12.80%. See Viper Energy Stock Buybacks on TipRanks.

Exxon Mobil (XOM) reported robust Q3 2026 profits, as its U.S. refineries boosted production to capitalize on the high diesel fuel prices sparked by the conflict in the Middle East. The oil and gas giant announced a quarterly dividend of $1.07 per share, payable on Dec. 10, reflecting a 3.9% increase. This marks Exxon's 44th consecutive year of dividend increases. At an annualized dividend of $4.28 per share, XOM offers a yield of 2.53%.

Impressed by the Q3 print, TD Cowen analyst Jason Gabelman reiterated a buy rating on Exxon stock and raised his price target to $193 from $180 to reflect higher downstream earnings, with the expectation of increased mid-cycle margins.

Gabelman noted that the strength in Exxon's refining business continues to offset Middle East-related headwinds. He added that the company increased its ownership in LNG projects, which should reduce its Middle East concentration over time from 75% to 50% and lower the likelihood of diversification-focused mergers and acquisitions. Beyond LNG, Gabelman expects Exxon's growth in the 2030s to come from Guyana, Kashagan, Permian, and possibly Venezuela.

Gabelman noted that Exxon announced a dividend hike of nearly 4% and plans to maintain buybacks at $20 billion for 2027. "The decision to maintain reduces the pro-cyclicality nature of the buyback," said the analyst.

Gabelman ranks No. 347 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been successful 66% of the time, delivering an average return of 16.90%. See Exxon Mobil Ownership Structure on TipRanks.

Williams (WMB), which owns and operates energy infrastructure to deliver natural gas, reported strong third-quarter results. The company announced a quarterly dividend of $0.525 per share, or $2.10 per share annualized, payable on Dec. 28. WMB offers a dividend yield of 3.09%.

In reaction to the Q3 2026 results, CIBC analyst Robert Catellier reaffirmed a buy rating on Williams stock with a price target of $82. The analyst noted that Q3 results were essentially in line with expectations and reflected continued strength in execution across WMB's infrastructure portfolio.

Catellier said that Williams' Q3 performance was driven by higher transmission, storage, and power generation revenues, as well as contributions from the Momentum Midstream acquisition.

He noted that Williams maintained its full-year EBITDA guidance of $8.3 billion to $8.5 billion. In fact, results are tracking toward the higher end of the guidance range, backed by contributions from recently completed projects and the Momentum Midstream deal. WMB continues to target long-term EBITDA compound annual growth rate of at least 11% through 2030.

Catellier expects WMB's long-term targets to be supported by incremental transmission and storage opportunities, including Transco's Southeast Supply Enhancement II project and an 84 MMcf/d expansion of the Garden Connector project.

#2808. Abdul Carter demanding more from himself after breakout may be key to Giants ‘super challenging’ Colts task

If Bruce Springsteen's go-to encore performance is "Born to Run," maybe Abdul Carter's can be "Born to Run Down QBs."

Carter, who did not practice Wednesday with the Giants because of a supposedly minor calf/ankle injury, raised the bar of possibilities Sunday with a breakout game (12 quarterback pressures, five quarterback hits and two sacks) followed by his self-description as "the best in the world."

So, what does the freshly named NFC Defensive Player of the Week have in store Sunday against the Colts and new starting quarterback Anthony Richardson Sr.?

"Something I said to the team during training camp: What this sport is all about each week is having to prove and re-prove, so gotta do it again," Carter said. "I could've done a lot better — get a couple more sacks — but we'll see."

"I think I can be the greatest," Carter said, "so I'm going to keep chasing that."

The Giants are going to be chasing Richardson, who will be making his first start since Dec. 22, 2024.

The 244-pound tackle-breaker is more dangerous on designed runs (RPOs, zone reads and even option keepers with Jonathan Taylor as a decoy) than drop-back passes (career 50.6 percent completion rate).

Abdul Carter reacts during the Giants' win over the Jaguars on Nov. 22, 2026, at MetLife Stadium. Getty Images

"I think we have an opportunity to leave a mark this week, earn another 'W,' extend our lead [in the NFC East]," said Carter, who declared himself "fine" for game day. "I attack each week the same way. Every team is different. But I'm going to prepare the same way I did last week — and do a little bit more."

The Colts reinstalled Richardson — a 2023 first-round draft bust — as their quarterback after surprisingly cutting Daniel Jones, who started the first 11 games.

"They are going to do things to make sure the game comes to him easily so he can get in a rhythm," safety Jevón Holland said. "His running style is like a Cam Newton — using his size to his advantage, stiff-arms, putting his shoulder down and getting extra yards. It's a challenge playing a quarterback with that size and speed."

The Colts quarterback change threw the Giants a "super challenging" curveball similar to Week 9, when they faced dual-threat Joe Milton III in his Eagles debut, head coach John Harbaugh said.

"It's a little better to see him the second game because you kind of see what they've got in for him," Harbaugh said. "We won't have the advantage of knowing what particular plays they choose to run.

"We watched all of his cut-ups — about 700 plays of his NFL career. He's one of a kind — a rare athlete. His career hasn't taken off for whatever reason, but that talent's there. He's going to be looking forward to his opportunity to prove himself, as is their whole team, so we're taking it very seriously."

Six of Carter's 12 pressures against the Jaguars came in less than 2.5 seconds, bringing his season total to 23 quick pressures (ranked No. 3 in the NFL). It certainly looked like he had a bead on the snap count, which spells trouble for a Colts offensive line pieced together after injuries and trade departures.

"That was crazy," 10-year veteran defensive lineman Dawuane Smoot, Carter's teammate for the last month, told The Post. "The amount of focus he had during the week, and I don't know what tip he had on that ball because he still hasn't even told us."

Smoot has played with future Hall of Famer Von Miller and Pro Bowler Josh Hines-Allen. What is Carter's ceiling?

Anthony Richardson Sr. throws a pass during the Colts' blowout loss to the Texans on Nov. 19, 2026, at Reliant Stadium in Houston. IMAGN IMAGES via Reuters Connect

"You can't really put a name on it," Smoot said. "He could be the greatest of all time. He has the confidence to be able to get there."

Richardson is trying to speak into existence salvaging his career. He will challenge the Giants safeties by dragging them up in support but using his big arm to throw over their heads.

"We're ball-conscious at all times," said Holland, who has four of the Giants' 10 interceptions. "He hasn't played, so we don't really know what his accuracy levels are. I'm going to expect him to deliver the ball and play my keys as such."

Unless, of course, Carter's pressure blows up the timing of plays again.

"If we are who we say we are," Carter said, "we have to … stop the run and earn the right to rush the passer."

#2801. Myles Garrett won’t be ‘100%’ for remainder of season

Myles Garrett is back to playing football in a Rams uniform for the third game in a row, but there is concern about what his long-term health will look like.

"It is unrealistic to think he will be 100% or even 90% this season," NFL insider Tom Pelissero reported.

"There is no looking back — he expects to keep ramping up his role," Pelissero said of Garrett.

Myles Garrett coming on the field during an NFL game. Getty Images

Garrett spoke more in depth about his injury on "The Rich Eisen Show" earlier this month and talked about how it is hard to come back "100% in the middle of a season."

"I was expecting to come back in a position where I wasn't going to do any further damage to my own health and longevity, but I could have a positive impact for my team and my teammates," Garrett said. "And that's fortunately what I was able to do my first game back."

The concern for Garrett's health came after he suffered a knee injury during their Week 1 loss to the 49ers in Melbourne, Australia.

Garrett played 40 snaps against San Francisco and rotated in and out of the team's defensive line. He finished the game with no tackles, sacks or quarterback hits.

Myles Garrett greeting a member of the Washington Commanders after an NFL game. Getty Images

The Rams' top offseason acquisition spent most of training camp sidelined due to knee soreness but played through it during the 49ers game.

Once the Rams returned from Australia, Garrett headed to the team's injured list and needed arthroscopic knee surgery, keeping him sidelined until Week 9 when they played the Commanders.

Myles Garrett sacking Commanders quarterback Jayden Daniels in his first NFL game back from arthroscopic surgery. AP Photo/Nick Wass

Garrett was expected to play about 25 snaps in his first game back, but he ended up exceeding that goal and played 35 of the Rams' 58 defensive plays.

His first game back was a statement performance, compiling three sacks and a forced fumble against Washington.

#2799. Federal Reserve Board announces pricing, effective January 1, 2027, for payment services the Federal Reserve Banks provide to banks and credit unions, and changes to streamline and adjust their check services

An official website of the United States Government

Official websites use .govA .gov website belongs to an official government organization in the United States.

Secure .gov websites use HTTPSA lock (LockLocked padlock icon) or https:// means you've safely connected to the .gov website. Share sensitive information only on official, secure websites.

The Federal Reserve, the central bank of the United States, provides the nation with a safe, flexible, and stable monetary and financial system.

Review of Monetary Policy Strategy, Tools, and Communications

Mergers, Acquisitions, and Other Applications

Financial Stability Coordination & Actions

Financial Market Utilities & Infrastructures

For release at 1:30 p.m. EST Share

The Federal Reserve Board on Thursday announced pricing, effective January 1, 2027, for payment services the Federal Reserve Banks provide to banks and credit unions, such as the clearing of checks, automated clearing house (ACH) transactions, instant payments, and wholesale payment and settlement services.

By law, the Federal Reserve must establish fees to recover the costs, including imputed costs, of providing payment services over the long run. The Federal Reserve expects to recover 101.5 percent of actual and imputed expenses in 2027, including the return on equity that would have been earned if a private-sector firm provided the services. Overall, price changes for 2027 will result in an estimated 1.4 percent average price increase for established, mature services.

In addition, the Federal Reserve Banks announced changes to streamline and adjust their check services. As described in the request for information on check services issued last year, the volume of checks processed by the Federal Reserve Banks is declining, but checks remain an important payment instrument for many consumers and businesses. The changes were informed by public feedback and ensure that the Federal Reserve Banks continue to provide check services efficiently. The adjustments include optimizing check processing windows during low-volume periods, increasing fees that banks and credit unions pay to process checks, and other operational updates. These changes do not impact consumers' and businesses' ability to pay with or receive checks and only impact how banks and credit unions process checks with Federal Reserve Banks.

The entire 2027 fee schedule for all of the Federal Reserve's payment services is published on FRBservices.org®. Additional information on the Federal Reserve's payments services, including service usage statistics, is available on the Board's website.

For media inquiries, please email [email protected] or call 202-452-2955.â¯â¯

#2796. Agencies extend comment period for proposed third-party risk management guidance

An official website of the United States Government

Official websites use .govA .gov website belongs to an official government organization in the United States.

Secure .gov websites use HTTPSA lock (LockLocked padlock icon) or https:// means you've safely connected to the .gov website. Share sensitive information only on official, secure websites.

The Federal Reserve, the central bank of the United States, provides the nation with a safe, flexible, and stable monetary and financial system.

Review of Monetary Policy Strategy, Tools, and Communications

Mergers, Acquisitions, and Other Applications

Financial Stability Coordination & Actions

Financial Market Utilities & Infrastructures

For release at 4:15 p.m. EST Share

The Federal Deposit Insurance Corporation, the Federal Reserve Board, the National Credit Union Administration, and the Office of the Comptroller of the Currency today announced that they will extend, until December 16, the comment period for proposed guidance on managing risks associated with third-party relationships. Comments on the proposal were originally due November 16.

The proposed guidance reflects the agencies' supervisory experience and lessons learned from examining financial institutions' third-party risk management practices. It is intended to help banks and credit unions tailor their risk management practices to the risks associated with individual third-party relationships. The proposed guidance takes a principles-based approach and, like all supervisory guidance, is non-binding.

When finalized, the federal bank regulatory agencies plan to rescind existing third-party risk management guidance and replace it with the finalized guidance to promote consistency and prudent innovation in the banking industry. NCUA also plans to rescind its existing third-party risk management guidance and will conduct its own reviews to update related guidance as needed.

Also today, the Federal Reserve Board separately announced that it will extend, until December 16, the comment period on a proposed third-party risk management guide specifically for Federal Reserve-supervised traditional community banks.

Federal Register notice: Proposed Third-Party Risk Management Guidance; Extension of Comment Period

Federal Register notice: Proposed Third-Party Risk Management Guide for Traditional Community Banking Organizations

Agencies seek comment on proposed third-party risk management guidance and issue statement on community bank engagement with core service providers

#2795. Seahawks-49ers Wednesday injury report: Sam Darnold practices fully

Coming off two consecutive wins and their bye, there's plenty working in favor of the Seattle Seahawks as they head into a pivotal matchup against the NFC-leading San Francisco 49ers on Sunday, but Wednesday's injury report wasn't exactly promising.

With the status of backups quarterbacks Drew Lock and Jalen Milroe up in the air, a full practice for Sam Darnold certainly bodes well for his return to come against the 49ers. During Monday's press conference, head coach Mike Macdonald refused to make any officials declarations regarding the Super Bowl winning quarterback, but said Wednesday's practice would be a big indicator of whether he'd be able to suit up, and it appears he's trending in the right direction.

Macdonald has not yet confirmed Darnold as the starter, but stated he remains the team's go-to guy when healthy and a quarterback change is not being contemplated.

Although Lock's right elbow tendinitis has lingered since October, he's suited up the past two weeks and his limited practice merely appears cautionary. Regarding Milroe, whether he's able to particpant in any capacity over the next two days will likely determine his availability against San Francisco, but the team has recently signed QB Adrian Martinez on their practice squad should he be sidelined this weekend.

Regardless of who's under center, they'll face an additional challenge with OG Quenton Nelson and OT Abraham Lucas ruled out, a move announced by Macdonald this afternoon. Josh Jones is set to draw the start in place of Lucas, with Anthony Bradford in line to suit up at right guard — if he's cleared.

Despite having played in Week 10 against the Las Vegas Raiders, the fourth-year pro was limited with a knee injury that forced him to practice in a limited capacity leading up to the game, and it appears to still be lingering. Bradford previously missed five weeks while on injured reserve as he dealt with a knee injury originally suffered in Week 2.

Should he be unable to go, rookie Beau Stephens would be in line to draw the start.

Arguably the two most troublesome absences' are that of DT Jarran Reed and LB Demarcus Lawrence. Reed originally suffered a toe injury against the Raiders but later returned to the game. It remains unknown if the team's decision to hold him out is cautionary or because it worsened, but his status tomorrow will definitely be an indicator for Sunday.

As for Lawrence, there hadn't been an indication he was dealing with an injury and the severity remains unclear.

Although DT Leonard Williams' limited practice is an improvement, WR Jaxon Smith-Njigba and LB Ernest Jones IV not participant in a full capacity is alarming. However, the veteran linebacker previously popped up on Seattle's practice report for Week 3 and 9 with separate injuries and went on to play — so his status downgrade shouldn't be flagged just yet.

The same goes for Smith-Njigba, who was flagged for an ankle injury prior to the Seahawks' Week 6 game against the Denver Broncos and went on to play without an injury designation.

Further updates should come tomorrow when Seattle holds their second practice of the week as well as when they release their official injury designations on Friday.

#2787. Tokenization platform Securitize gains 10% postmarket on Q3 results, move into asset-backed finance

Tokenization platform Securitize climbed about 10% postmarket after reporting third quarter growth alongside increased operating expenses, and announcing an expansion into asset-backed finance through a strategic acquisition.

Miami-based Securitize, which made its public market debut in July, operates in the relatively new corner of the crypto equity universe involved in institutional tokenization infrastructure. Tokenized assets on the platform grew 9% in the third quarter, to $4.8 billion, from the same period a year ago. Aggregate transaction volume rose 20% from a year ago, to $4.3 billion. Revenue of $11.5 million was up 28% from last year.

The growth came as bullish sentiment around tokenization gained traction throughout the third quarter, ahead of the Security and Exchange Commission formalizing the long awaited Innovation Exemption on Sept. 17, which created a temporary pathway for trading of certain tokenized stocks and strengthened the regulatory outlook.

Profitability is still meaningfully below year-ago levels, due in part to costs associated with going public over the summer, as well as the scaling up of the tokenization business at an active time for the entire sector, Securitize CEO Carlos Domingo told CNBC in an exclusive interview.

Securitize reinforced "certain areas of the company like finance, compliance, legal [and] cybersecurity," which increased corporate costs "compared to a year ago when we were privately held," Domingo said. "We're in in a very good position. The adjusted EBITDA … is improving significantly from previous quarters."

Securitize reported an adjusted EBITDA loss of $2 million, compared with positive adjusted EBITDA of $3.6 million a year ago, better than a $5.5 million loss in the second quarter. The company posted a 183% increase in operating costs and expenses.

Also on Monday, the Securitize board authorized a $50 million share buyback and announced the acquisition of River One, an institutional asset-backed financing platform developing technology to connect stablecoin capital with traditional lending markets. Terms were not disclosed.

Securitize sees an opportunity to modernize the $9 trillion market for asset backed finance by using stablecoins to finance real world assets, generate yield and tokenize investment positions and credit products, Domingo said.The acquisition also marks an expansion beyond Securitize's core niche markets at a time when tokenization's competitive landscape is growing, with traditional asset managers like BlackRock and Franklin Templeton, and established financial market infrastructure providers like DTCC and Broadridge, entering the industry.

"We have been very active in tokenized funds, treasuries [and] yield … And we've been obviously recently very active in tokenized stocks, so this adds another piece of the puzzle in the emerging tokenization space for us to capture revenue growth."

#2783. Blake Corum receives a heartwarming birthday surprise from his family moments before Thanksgiving Eve game

The Los Angeles Rams and Green Bay Packers are about to play in the first-ever Thanksgiving Eve game, and the calendar just happened to have it land on Blake Corum's birthday.

While Corum took the field at SoFi Stadium for pregame warmups, the Rams running back had a moment he'll never forget.

Blake Corum running past a Giants defender in an NFL game. Charles Wenzelberg/New York Post

Corum was seen walking toward the end zone and was surprised by members of his family who showed up wearing matching sweaters that included Corum's face while he was wearing a birthday party hat on his head.

Corum's family members were also holding individual signs that matched the face on their sweaters to show their support for the Rams running back.

Before the Rams went on the bye week last week, Corum had his best performance of the season, when he was the Rams' leading rusher in their 34-6 win against the Cardinals.

Blake Corum celebrating a play during an NFL game. AP Photo/Stephanie Scarbrough

Los Angeles ran the ball for 202 yards, and Corum was their lead running back, carrying the ball 17 times for 100 yards and a touchdown.

Corum is in his third season in the NFL and has shared lead bell-cow duties with Kyren Williams. The duo has nearly matched each other's production entirely this year, as they've combined for 1,163 yards and 11 touchdowns.

Blake Corum running past a Washington Commanders defender during an NFL game. Getty Images

Corum comes into Wednesday's game, scoring a touchdown in four consecutive games.

#2782. Federal Reserve announces College Fed Challenge winners

An official website of the United States Government

Secure .gov websites use HTTPSA lock (LockLocked padlock icon) or https:// means you've safely connected to the .gov website. Share sensitive information only on official, secure websites.

The Federal Reserve, the central bank of the United States, provides the nation with a safe, flexible, and stable monetary and financial system.

Review of Monetary Policy Strategy, Tools, and Communications

Mergers, Acquisitions, and Other Applications

Financial Stability Coordination & Actions

Financial Market Utilities & Infrastructures

For release at 4:15 p.m. EST Share

The University of California, Los Angeles won the 23rd Annual National College Fed Challenge.

The University of California, Los Angeles (UCLA) won the 23rd annual national College Fed Challenge on Friday, a team competition that encourages undergraduate students to learn about the U.S. economy, monetary policymaking, and the role of the Federal Reserve System. Teams analyze economic and financial conditions and formulate a monetary policy recommendation, modeling the Federal Open Market Committee. The team, from Los Angeles, California, represented the San Francisco District and included Aarushi Maheshwari, Annika Chan, Atul Prasanth, Caitlyn Cox, Finn Karish, Eli Brooks (alternate), Kennedy Yang (alternate), Manasi Vartak (alternate), Ronojoy Borpujari (alternate), and Sahil Sudani (alternate). The team's advisers were Chris Surro and Ali Haider Ismail.

"I am very happy to congratulate the students who competed in this year's College Fed Challenge, especially the winners and finalists," said Federal Reserve Chairman Kevin Warsh. "They took on the same big questions we policymakers face. I'm impressed by their talent, their rigor, and their eagerness to think hard about monetary policy and the economy."

This year's College Fed Challenge included 141 schools from 34 states across the nation, the most in the competition's history. Teams submitted video presentations or participated in local competitions last month. Twenty-one semi-finalist teams then participated in question-and-answer sessions held earlier this month, and seven finalists were invited to the Federal Reserve Board for the national finals. The other national finalists were the University of Chicago in second place and the University of Maryland, College Park in third place. Teams with honorable mentions include Cleveland State University, Harvard College, Pennsylvania State University, and St. Lawrence University.

Teams were evaluated on their economic analysis, responses to the judges' questions, teamwork, and presentations.

#2780. What we learned as Kings can't overcome brutal first quarter in loss to Warriors

What we learned as Kings can't overcome brutal first quarter in loss to Warriors originally appeared on NBC Sports Bay Area

SAN FRANCISCO, Calif. — The Sacramento Kings were doomed from the start.

After falling behind by 25 in the first quarter Wednesday night to the Golden State Warriors, they fought back to make the game respectable in the third quarter. But the Warriors ultimately pulled away — even Stephen Curry out with an oblique injury — and won comfortably.

De'Anthony Melton made eight 3s while scoring 24 points, and the Warriors won 129-110 at Chase Center. The Kings fell to 2-16 and will have three days off, including Thanksgiving on Thursday, before hosting a back-to-back against the Minnesota Timberwolves on Sunday and the New Orleans Pelicans on Monday.

Wednesday's loss was the Kings' final game of NBA Cup group play. Sacramento went 0-4 and won't make it out of the group stage after benig outscored by 49 points.

The Kings were missing Zach LaVine, who was out with knee soreness coming out of Monday's loss to the San Antonio Spurs.

The game got off to a terrible start for Sacramento, and they couldn't recover.

The Warriors scored 88 points in their win Sunday over the Boston Celtics, and they had half that total in the first quarter on Wednesday thanks to the Kings' horrendous defense. It was the most for Golden State in any quarter all season.

Golden State went 9-of-13 from deep in the opening frame, taking advantage of open looks repeatedly, while the Kings missed all nine of their attempts from distance. That 27-0 advantage from 3-point range led to Sacramento trailing 44-19.

The Warriors were without Curry, but Melton did his best to impersonate the NBA's best shooter. He had 12 points while making four 3s in his first eight minutes, while Santos matched him with 12 points in the opening period.

The Kings had 10 first-quarter points from Domantas Sabonis, but none of his teammates had more than four. Sacramento's bench was outscored 19-0, and they allowed 15 points off their eight turnovers.

The Kings weren't going to let their first-quarter performance define the game.

They opened the second half with far more energy than the first, and that showed up with its 3-point shooting. Sacramento began the third on a 25-10 run to cut the lead to four, while making 6 of 9 from distance.

However, Golden State ended the quarter on a 19-11 run and took a 12-point lead into the fourth.

The Kings came into the game with the NBA's second-worst plus-minus in third quarter, but they outscored the Warriors 36-29 in the third Wednesday; Sacramento held a 70-57 advantage across the second and third quarters.

The Kings' top players approximated their usual scoring. Sabonis had 27 points, with 10 rebounds and six assists. Keegan Murray had 18 points, and Darius Acuff Jr. added 22.

But the bench scoring favored Golden State 61-19.

Sacramento came into the game with the lowest-scoring bench in the NBA, averaging just 23.4 points per game while the Warriors ranked second at 44.7. Murray had to play 42 minutes. Malik Monk had just five points in 13 minutes.

Precious Achiuwa finished with 11 points, his highest-scoring game since Oct. 29. He missed three games recently with a lower leg injury. Emanuel Sharp started in place of LaVine and finished with nine points on 3-of-6 shooting from 3. He fouled out with 1:54 remaining after playing 25 minutes.

#2775. Agencies announce dollar thresholds for smaller loan exemption from appraisal requirements for higher-priced mortgage loans

An official website of the United States Government

Official websites use .govA .gov website belongs to an official government organization in the United States.

Secure .gov websites use HTTPSA lock (LockLocked padlock icon) or https:// means you've safely connected to the .gov website. Share sensitive information only on official, secure websites.

The Federal Reserve, the central bank of the United States, provides the nation with a safe, flexible, and stable monetary and financial system.

Review of Monetary Policy Strategy, Tools, and Communications

Mergers, Acquisitions, and Other Applications

Financial Stability Coordination & Actions

Financial Market Utilities & Infrastructures

For release at 3:00 p.m. EST Share

The Consumer Financial Protection Bureau, the Federal Reserve Board, and the Office of the Comptroller of the Currency today announced that the 2027 threshold for higher-priced mortgage loans that are subject to special appraisal requirements will increase from $34,200 to $35,500.

The threshold amount will be effective January 1, 2027, and is based on the 3.9 percent annual percentage increase in the Consumer Price Index for Urban Wage Earners and Clerical Workers, known as CPI-W, as of June 1, 2026.

The Dodd-Frank Act added special appraisal requirements for higher-priced mortgage loans to the Truth in Lending Act, including that creditors obtain a written appraisal based on a physical visit to the interior of the home before making a higher-priced mortgage loan. The rules implementing these requirements contain an exemption for loans at or below a threshold amount that is adjusted annually to reflect CPI-W increases.

Federal Register notice: Appraisals for Higher-Priced Mortgage Loans Exemption Threshold (PDF)

#2772. Agencies announce dollar thresholds for applicability of truth in lending and consumer leasing rules for consumer credit and lease transactions

An official website of the United States Government

Official websites use .govA .gov website belongs to an official government organization in the United States.

Secure .gov websites use HTTPSA lock (LockLocked padlock icon) or https:// means you've safely connected to the .gov website. Share sensitive information only on official, secure websites.

The Federal Reserve, the central bank of the United States, provides the nation with a safe, flexible, and stable monetary and financial system.

Review of Monetary Policy Strategy, Tools, and Communications

Mergers, Acquisitions, and Other Applications

Financial Stability Coordination & Actions

Financial Market Utilities & Infrastructures

For release at 3:00 p.m. EST Share

The Federal Reserve Board and the Consumer Financial Protection Bureau today announced the dollar thresholds used to determine whether certain consumer credit and lease transactions in 2027 are subject to certain protections under Regulation Z (Truth in Lending) and Regulation M (Consumer Leasing).

By law, the agencies are required to adjust the thresholds annually based on the annual percentage increase in the Consumer Price Index for Urban Wage Earners and Clerical Workers, known as CPI-W. Transactions at or below the thresholds are subject to the protections of the regulations.

Specifically, based on the 3.9 percent annual percentage increase in the CPI-W as of June 1, 2026, Regulation Z and Regulation M generally will apply to consumer credit transactions and consumer leases of $76,300 or less in 2027. However, private education loans and loans secured by real property, such as mortgages, are subject to Regulation Z regardless of the amount of the loan.

Federal Register notice: Consumer Leasing (Regulation M) (PDF)

Federal Register notice: Truth in Lending (Regulation Z) (PDF)

Board of Governors of the Federal Reserve System

#2771. The Aaron Rodgers ultimatum that sent the Steelers into chaos

Aaron Rodgers ended up on the wrong side of his own ultimatum.

The now-former Steelers quarterback was waived by the team on Wednesday, following a wild 18-hour period that saw him get benched in a late-night announcement and then go nuclear on coach Mike McCarthy in a nationally televised tirade.

It all started with a Tuesday meeting between the coach and QB, who won a Super Bowl together in 2011 with the Packers.

"I didn't want this to be the way that it ended," Rodgers said in an explosive appearance on the "Pat McAfee Show" on Wednesday. "But I was very clear with the ultimatum and it wasn't a veiled ultimatum. It wasn't posturing. It was me as a leader of the football team inspired by my teammates who talk about this all the time. Literally all the time in council meetings, on group chats, in the locker room, just some of the things that are just like wow, how is this the reality we're dealing with? I was, in my professional opinion based on 22 years of experience … the only person who could talk to him and be honest with him to tell him that what's best for the team is for him to not call the plays and if he wasn't up for that, they should probably release me."

According to Netflix NFL insider Tom Pelissero, McCarthy was open to Rodgers' demand, as the veteran has been calling plays himself in recent weeks, but other coaches and "some in the locker room" convinced him it was not a move they wanted.

McCarthy confirmed in a press conference later in the day that Rodgers attempted to put the screws to him.

Mike McCarthy and Aaron Rodgers speak on the sidelines as the Steelers face the Bengals. Getty Images

"We got to a point, we talked some things — you know communication and things like that — and there was an ultimatum made clearly outside of his job description and so it was my decision to bench him," McCarthy told reporters.

McCarthy made the call to move backup Mason Rudolph into the starter's role, kicking off a chaotic day in which Rodgers accused his coach of falling asleep in meetings and divulging a halftime gaffe in which he said, "1-2-3 Packers!" among other things.

Pittsburgh players reportedly watched Rodgers burn it all down on McAfee's live ESPN broadcast on their phones in the locker room in real time.

Hours later, the team granted the soon-to-be 43-year-old quarterback his ultimatum and sent him packing.

#2767. ‘Right Behind Skip (Bayless) on Top LeBron (James) Haters,’ ‘Needs To Retire’ — NBA Fans Deliver Blunt Verdict on Charles Barkley’s 76ers Championship Take

Charles Barkley cast doubt on the Philadelphia 76ers' NBA championship chances Wednesday, pointing to the steady stream of games Joel Embiid, LeBron James, and Jaylen Brown have missed this season.

The 76ers legend's skepticism comes amid a strong start for Philadelphia, which improved to an Eastern Conference-best 15-4 following Wednesday's 111-104 home win over the Miami Heat.

Fans were quick to push back, both questioning Barkley's feelings toward James and his former team and taking shots at his ringless career.

Despite Philadelphia's early-season success, its star-studded core has been in and out of the lineup. James has missed four games, Brown has sat out three, and Tyrese Maxey has missed one. Meanwhile, Embiid has been absent for five contests, missing the last two due to an illness, including Wednesday's victory that snapped Miami's seven-game winning streak.

The 76ers have now gone 6-1 since their Big 4 last suited up together on Nov. 10. While James and Embiid were expected to be tightly managed entering the season due to their age and injury concerns, Barkley isn't sold.

"I don't even know how to judge this Sixers team," Barkley said on Wednesday's ESPN broadcast. "They got a lot of talent, but if they're gonna be missing games throughout the year, No. 1, Kenny [Smith's] right, they need to find another big guy when Embiid's out. But I don't even know how I can judge them."

Jabari Walker has started in Embiid's place in each of the last two outings, with Dominick Barlow and Adem Bona contributing off the bench, leaving Philadelphia undersized.

READ MORE: 'No Wonder He Never Won a Ring,' 'Brain Dead Take' — NBA Fans Rip Charles Barkley's 'Unbelievably Unserious' VJ Edgecombe Trade Idea

"I don't care how gaudy their record is because Embiid's not playing this game, [other nights] LeBron's not playing, Jaylen Brown's not playing. If they're going to do this, I don't think you can win a championship," Barkley continued. "Listen, I could be wrong. I don't think you can just mix and match and get through the playoffs."

The Hall of Famer then pointed to the importance of team chemistry and solidified rotations come the postseason:

"When they get in the playoffs, when everybody's made their trades, and they got their normal rotations, I don't think they can win a championship … You just don't show up and win a championship."

Several fans tied their reactions to James, who is in his first season with the 76ers, suggesting Barkley's verdict has more to do with the 22-time All-Star than with the roster around him.

"He's right behind Skip [Bayless] on top LeBron haters," one fan asserted.

Another argued Barkley's criticism followed James from the Los Angeles Lakers:

"As soon as LeBron left, he started glazing the Lakers though after years of hate."

A third fan echoed that sentiment, suggesting, "He's retired from hating the Lakers. It's the Sixers now. All because of one man."

One fan connected the dots to Barkley's own time in Philadelphia:

"He don't like the Sixers, he never did. He didn't like it before he was drafted, while he was playing, or after he finished playing."

Others zeroed in on the 1993 MVP's résumé.

"It's something about someone sitting up talking about championships that has never won one and actually played in the league… He needs to retire," one fan wrote.

Another asked, "How do you know what it takes to win a championship if you've never won?"

Following Wednesday's win, the 76ers (15-4) sit two and a half games ahead of the Boston Celtics (12-6) and New York Knicks (12-6) for the East's best record.

Still, it appears Barkley will need to see Philadelphia's stars share the floor more consistently before buying into his old team.

This article originally appeared on Pro Football Network.

#2765. What's ahead for AI financing after Nvidia CEO Jensen Huang departs

Nvidia CEO Jensen Huang's departure hasn't produced a broad shock to the credit market, but it is putting the company's role as a financier of artificial intelligence infrastructure under scrutiny.

Huang, who stepped down Thursday amid an espionage and adultery scandal, built Nvidia into an AI powerhouse in the 33 years since he co-founded the company. Chief financial officer Colette Kress has been named interim CEO as the company searches for a permanent chief executive.

Nvidia not only supplies its graphics processing units to hyperscalers and tech giants, it also provides financing to other companies to help the industry expand. That could all change.

"Although we expect the company to fulfill all of its previously announced commitments, we believe that going forward, it may be more difficult for the company to negotiate new multi-party investments among ecosystem partners at the same scale it has done up to this point," Goldman Sachs analyst James Schneider said in a note Thursday.

The news of Huang's resignation comes after investors were already concerned about the massive amount of debt being taken on to fund AI infrastructure.

Tech bond yields have risen and spreads have widened on credit default swaps, most notably in lower-rated names. Credit default swaps are contracts in which investors pay for protection in case the borrower can't repay its debt. Wider spreads indicate investors view the bonds as riskier.

On Friday, the yield on Nvidia's 4.5% bond due in 2031 sat at 5.717%, nearly 5% higher week to date. Bond yields move inversely to prices.

Tech stocks slumped Friday with the Nasdaq 100 closing down 1.2% as markets continued to digest the Nvidia news. Yields on hyperscaler bonds ticked slightly higher on Friday, signaling the broader concern about AI debt lingers.

It's the smaller AI companies that could ultimately see an impact if Nvidia makes any changes to its AI financing. The company had $36 billion in off-balance-sheet AI cloud agreements, or backstops, for neoclouds, according to its last quarterly financial filing in July.

Neoclouds CoreWeave, Nebius and IREN are all in Nvidia's portfolio. Their shares were down sharply Thursday.

CoreWeave, which has become the poster child for AI debt fears, saw its bond yields retreat after its quarterly earnings on Tuesday eased investor concerns. The yield on its 9.75% bond due in 2031 was 14.79% on Friday after climbing to 16.7% last week. The spread on its five-year CDS also dropped more than 17% week to date, although it remained above 1,000 — which is generally considered a sign of distress.

It remains unclear how Nvidia's support for those companies may change, said CreditSights analyst Andy Li.

"We don't know what the ultimate strategy for Nvidia is going to be coming out the other side," he said.

"However, I think directionally, just given the fact that Jensen was so crucial in selling the Nvidia story and being a very aggressive deal maker, it's likely that a lot of this creative deal-making and circular financing and equity and credit support is going to moderate," he added.

CoreWeave CEO Michael Intrator told CNBC Wednesday, before Huang's departure, that Nvidia "is not one person."

"There are thousands of people over there that interface with thousands of people at my company every day. They integrate on the technology. They integrate on the physical infrastructure. They integrate on the networking," he said in an interview with "Squawk on the Street." "They have been a great partner for us for years. They will continue to be a great partner for us."

Bob Lang, founder and chief strategist at Explosive Options, doesn't see any big changes in terms of Nvidia's relevance or its ability to finance for now. As CFO, Kress was involved in the financing deals, and the search for a permanent CEO is going to take some time, he noted.

The real issue is just how much money the company will want to lend for AI financing, rather than, say using its hundreds of billions in free cash flow for other purposes, like share buybacks, capex, or acquisitions.

"Big companies like Nvidia and Meta, they're holding all the cards," he said. "When they decide that the game is over, that's it — it's going to be curtains for some of these smaller companies."

— CNBC'S Kif Leswing and Isabel O'Brien contributed reporting.

#2763. Warriors' offense, sparked by De'Anthony Melton, kicks into gear in win over Kings

Golden State Warriors guard Brandon Williams would know better than anyone that what De'Anthony Melton is doing is not quite normal. A month ago - posed a question as to whether Melton ever shot like this in high school - Williams curled up his nose in mock confusion.

"Melt?" said Williams, Melton's former teammate at Crespi High in Los Angeles, after the Warriors' win over the Clippers on Nov. 1. "F- no."

Melton is the inverse, now, of what he was. He rose to prominence in Southern California high school ball as a sort-of first-option Gary Payton II, a flasher and cutter and mid-post operator who would do everything on a basketball court without much being able to shoot. Years later, the Warriors signed Melton in 2024 because he could shoot. Nobody can quite appreciate that turnaround like Williams.

"For him to come here, and start raining threes," Williams said, "I'm like, 'This must be a Warriors thing, bruh.'"

This was a Warriors thing again on Wednesday night, in a 129-110 win over Sacramento. From Melton, who tied a career high with eight threes in a 24-point eruption. From - well, everyone, as a hapless Kings defense decided to duck under screens time and time again. And a lid that had sealed off the rim for Golden State for five straight games popped off, as a 27% clip from the three-point line in five previous games flipped into a 20-for-44 night from downtown against the Kings.

Melton, who'd gone 1-for-12 across his previus three games, caught fire early and nailed a couple more fourth-quarter triples. Reserve Georges Niang, in an utter dry spell, hit three threes. And Gui Santos, banged up for much of the start to this season, finished with a season-best 22 points.

3-Point Goals: 14-40, .350 (Hunter 3-6, Sharp 3-6, Acuff Jr. 3-12, Murray 2-6, Sabonis 1-1, Achiuwa 1-2, Black 1-5, Monk 0-2).

Golden State will release an update Friday after re-evaluating star Stephen Curry, who now has missed six games while rehabbing a torn oblique abdominal muscle. Their consistent struggles with health, though, have gone much deeper than simply missing Curry - or even Jimmy Butler, Moses Moody and Kristaps Porzingis. Slowly, and not a moment too soon, these Warriors are returning to some partial semblance of full strength.

At the start of the second quarter, backup point guard Brandon Williams touched the floor in his first action since a hamstring strain suffered in a Nov. 9 win over the Pelicans. For years, through chronic knee issues earlier in his youth and heaps of thankless buckets in the G League, Williams has kept his motor running by simply wanting to hoop. He is a self-made Southern California grinder, getting up hundreds of shots in his old high school gym this summer while waiting for a veteran-minimum deal that took a long while to come.

For two weeks, he sat on Golden State's bench, nursing that hamstring and stewing at the sight of an offense that failed to generate much of any dribble penetration without him.

"After it happened - the first couple days, I didn't really do too much, so I'm just itching to play basketball," Williams said Tuesday, recounting the hamstring injury. "And everybody here knows I love basketball. So if I get on the court, I probably - may be doing a little bit too much, and they'll drag me off of it."

The very instant the Warriors lifted the red flag on Williams on Wednesday night, he hit the gas, the trait that changed the dynamics of Golden State's non-Curry units early this season. Just 30 seconds into his return, he down-shifted into eighth gear for a driving layup. And 20 seconds after that, he hit full turbo, turning purple Sacramento jerseys into mere blurs in an and-one transition finish as Melton scrunched his nose in gleeful disgust from the bench.

The rust, now, didn't shake this fast. After a rip-roaring 44-point first quarter - their highest output of the season - a suddenly high-octane Golden State offense began spinning its gears, as Williams found no traction on congested drives to the rim. An 0-for-5 stretch for the human teleporter beget a backslide for the Warriors and a sudden magnet-ball third quarter for Sacramento, as the Kings hit seven threes in the first eight minutes of the second half to cut the Warriors' lead, which once was 29, to four.

Williams' ability to single-handedly fight the tide, though - an element Golden State hasn't had with Curry down in a stretch of sheer offensive futility - loomed large here. He flew past a Kings turnstile for a late third-quarter layup, sneering as Sacramento called a timeout. Two assists came in quick succession for a Niang three and a Payton layup, and Golden State suddenly had a cushion again that it wouldn't relinquish.

#2762. Trump’s meddling in Huang scandal reignites voter angst over tech’s outsized political power

When voters headed to the polls and flipped Congress this month, they sent a clear message to Washington: They wanted change.

Voters wanted curbs on the technology industry's outsized power in Washington and CEOs cozying up to President Donald Trump to sway politics in their favor.

The Monday reinstatement of Nvidia CEO Jensen Huang at Trump's urging may be exactly the opposite of what voters voiced with their ballots.

"This whole ordeal only reinforces what Americans already believe about a broken political system that stands up for Big Tech CEOs and not the American people," Sacha Haworth, founder and CEO of The Tech Oversight Project, told CNBC.

Republicans are warning that Trump's unwavering support for the embattled artificial intelligence chip kingpin could further alienate voters.

"The data were so clear in the election that people don't trust big tech. The corruption message, I think, resonated a lot for Democrats in the election, and I think this sort of thing isn't helpful to Republicans in Congress who are going to be running again in two years," said Brendan Steinhauser, a GOP strategist and CEO of The Alliance for Secure AI Action, who had warned that AI data centers could be a "sleeper issue" in the lead-up to the 2026 midterms.

Before and after Election Day, voters were clear about their concern, which centered on data centers.

A Pew Research Center survey published in October found that 44% of voters considered AI very important to their vote. An NBC News exit poll found that 56% of voters said AI and data centers were crucial or a major factor in their vote for Congress. And in states including Texas, data center opposition forced GOP lawmakers who once welcomed development to change their positions.

There's a lot for tech CEOs to cheer under Trump, who was welcomed with open arms following a period of tough enforcement under President Joe Biden, and many reasons to align with the administration, said Sarah Kreps, a political scientist and professor at Cornell University who studies the intersection of technology and national security.

Chief among the reasons for celebration are the market's AI-driven gains since Trump's 2025 inauguration and his light-touch approach to tech regulation. The Nasdaq has gained nearly 21% from Jan. 17, 2025, the last trading day before the inauguration, and Tuesday's close.

"In the end, this looks like a calculated risk that the people and interests Trump prioritizes benefit more from a stable, rising market than the administration loses from the optics," Kreps said of Huang's reinstatement, adding that the Republican Party will be left to "bear the consequences" in 2028.

Outside the rarefied air of Silicon Valley and the White House, there is scant support for Huang's comeback after a series of extramarital affairs, including one with a suspected Chinese spy that critics say could have jeopardized national security and undermined the U.S.'s advantage in the AI race.

Trump sought to allay those fears this week when he told CNBC that he assured the board that Huang posed no national security threat. Instead, Trump used concerns over the CEO giving his talents to China as a major rationale behind the reinstatement push.

"The bigger threat would be losing the brain power of somebody like Jensen," Trump told CNBC this week. "That would be a threat to me. That would be a far bigger threat than anything we're talking about."

Haworth said the reinstatement raises concerns over how Huang's access to Trump benefits the company's business interests, especially as the U.S. investigates chip smuggling into China.

At the same time, Secretary of Commerce Howard Lutnick's perceived slowness to clamp down on illegal advanced AI chip sales to China, as well as his family's financial ties to Nvidia via Cantor Fitzgerald, the financial firm his sons now run, have drawn further congressional scrutiny.

"Voters are struggling with the high cost of living. They're angry about the corruption they see and a system that has been working more and more for the wealthy and well-connected, and making it harder and harder on working families," Rep. Suzan DelBene, D-Wash., chair of the Democratic Congressional Campaign Committee told CNBC.

"Corruption is an affordability issue. When they see money and favors going in places that don't help support working families, that's an affordability issue for them," DelBene said.

As Republicans try to rebuild after being routed in the midterms, the kind of leniency Trump has shown to Huang could backfire, GOP strategists told CNBC.

"Voters are tired of 'rules for thee, but not for me.' Most Americans have to live with the consequences of their decisions," said Brittany Martinez, executive director of Principles First, a center-right grassroots advocacy organization. "If you're a billionaire with the president's ear, apparently the White House will help you get your job back. That's exactly the kind of double standard that makes people wonder whose side this administration is on."

Marc Short, former chief of staff to Vice President Mike Pence and chair of Advancing American Freedom, the conservative think tank Pence founded, told CNBC that the Trump administration's involvement in Huang's reinstatement "sends a very terrible signal."

"I think it's consistent with the administration taking ownership in 41 separate private companies, to now feel that they can determine to the board who should be the CEO of one of America's biggest companies," he said.

#2760. Is a bottom in for the Nasdaq-100? Charts to watch

(See the video above for Frank's complete chart breakdown.)

Is the Nasdaq-100's recent bounce a sign that the bottom is in? We're going to look at several charts to help answer that question.

First, let's remember how we got here. In September, the NDX broke out from a bullish cup-and-handle pattern, producing a 33,200-upside target. That breakout quickly failed, however, and gave way to a bearish pattern that broke down aggressively two weeks ago.

The decline brought the index relatively close to its 26,350 downside target before the current rebound began.

Zooming out, it's telling to see where the bounce originated. Three important forms of support converged in the 25,000-26,000 area:

This confluence of support helped encourage buying after a roughly 15% decline.

For perspective, we also see what larger drawdowns of 20%, 30%, 40%, and 50% would look like … a lot worse.

The following chart tracks NDX drawdowns from 52-week highs going back to just after the financial crisis. Only a handful of declines exceeded 20%, and three produced relatively quick reversals: late 2018, the Covid lows in 2020 and April 2025.

Historically, buying into declines of approximately 15%–25% has generally paid off, even when additional downside occurred first (yellow highlights).

The chart also highlights previous declines of 10%–16% in green, putting the current pullback into perspective.

The major exception was 2022, when the NDX eventually declined nearly 36% over approximately 10 months ... a much worse outcome, which many investors are concerned could happen again now.

That raises the key question: Are we experiencing another routine correction, or the early stages of a larger bear market?

One important clue will be how far this bounce progresses.

Any rally that fails to reclaim the previous highs could establish a significant lower high. Additional lower highs would provide stronger evidence of a developing, longer downtrend.

We saw this in March 2022, when that first sharp rebound effort failed to reach the previous high before a much more severe decline followed.

The bottom line: The current bounce is encouraging, particularly given where it originated and knowing how well buying prior 10-20% drawdowns has paid off.

From here, it's all about the follow through.  We don't need to see new highs right away, but any rally from here that's met with another wave of strong selling could reignite downside momentum.

Frank Cappelleri is the founder and president of CappThesis, an independent research firm that helps active investors through time-tested chart and statistical analysis. Prior to starting CappThesis, he spent 25 years on Wall Street as an equity sales trader, technical analyst, research sales specialist and desk strategist.

#2758. NBA announces $11 billion investment plan to develop, modernize arenas for NBA Europe

Is European basketball ready for modern American-style NBA arenas? With adjacent entertainment districts around them?

The NBA announced plans Wednesday for a €10 billion (nearly $11 million at current exchange rates) initiative to pay for "the development and renovation of basketball arenas – and the sports and entertainment districts around them – across Europe." To make this happen, the NBA assembled a group of heavy-hitter global investment firms including Apollo Sports Capital; Ares Management; Arctos, a business of KKR; Blackstone; HPS Investment Partners, a part of BlackRock; J.P. Morgan; Sixth Street and Stonepeak, among others.

"We've seen firsthand how state-of-the-art arenas and the related developments around them can transform basketball teams and their surrounding communities," NBA Commissioner Adam Silver said in a statement announcing the initiative. "As we prepare to launch a new league in Europe alongside FIBA, we see a significant opportunity to help clubs develop facilities which both build on the unique traditions that define European basketball while also elevating the fan experience and attracting many more live events."

This is about arenas for NBA Europe teams, the league the NBA and partner FIBA plan to at least soft launch next October. That said, this initiative is entirely separate from the team investment process.

League officials have been open from the start that they see opportunity in Europe, not just because of the fan base, but because the arena infrastructure there does not maximize profitability — the NBA believes European clubs are leaving money on the table. Part of the plan to make NBA Europe profitable is to change that.

In its announcement, the NBA put it this way: "The investors will look to partner with participating clubs, ownership groups, local developers and other service providers to develop world-class arenas that improve the fan experience and, where appropriate, mixed-use districts around them. Depending on local needs and opportunities, these projects could include housing, workplaces, restaurants, retail and public spaces, helping to revitalize communities where people live, work and play. "

The NBA has long championed the idea that new world-class arenas drive economic growth in the cities where they are placed, not only through ticket sales and jobs created, but also by revitalizing parts of a city if there are housing and entertainment districts (with restaurants, theaters and more) around them. However, multiple studies suggest this is more a redistribution of wealth and spending from other parts of a city to the new district.

Europe does not have many arenas like the ones NBA teams often play in. The target cities for NBA Europe include Paris, Rome, London, Manchester, Lyon, Madrid, Barcelona, Athens, Berlin and more. However, those cities have not been finalized as the bidding process continues.

#2757. Rams S Kam Kinchens ejected; Packers WR Christian Watson evaluated for concussion

In the second quarter of the Green Bay Packers-Los Angeles Rams game, Rams safety Kam Kinchens was ejected for leaving his feet and leading with his helmet on a tackle attempt on Packers receiver Christian Watson. You can find video of the play below:

Initially, a cart came out for Watson, but the receiver was able to leave the field under his own power. The Packers are currently listing Watson as questionable to return and say that he's being evaluated for a concussion. To be honest, it looks like the biggest hit he ended up taking was when his head hit the turf on the way down, not necessarily Kinchens' blow to Watson's facemask.

Green Bay was not awarded a first down on this play due to offsetting penalties. Packers receiver Matthew Golden was called for offensive pass interference (you can only set a pick within a yard of the line of scrimmage), so they simply replayed the fourth-and-one play. After not getting a man coverage look with their running back's motion, like they had on the play that Watson was injured on, head coach Matt LaFleur called a timeout and elected to kick the football, setting up a 17-17 tie going into halftime.

We'll keep you updated when we hear more news on the Packers' injury situation.

Update: The Packers have now officially ruled Watson out with a concussion.

#2756. Bullish hopes spring as traders bet against a higher VIX: Options Action Live

With stocks mounting a comeback Wednesday, options trading across a few key volatility products suggests big swings in stocks may dampen in the near term.

More than 80,000 options traded on the Barclays S&P 500 VIX Short Term Futures ETN (VXX), almost three times the 30-day average, with more puts trading than calls, according to LiveVol data. The fund aims to track the moves in Cboe's VIX Index, which surged to 35 early Wednesday but slipped to below 28 as the S&P 500 rallied to within 25 basis points of unchanged.

At the same time, options volume on VIX itself was 50% above average, with an even more distinctive bias towards puts, suggesting traders see stocks firming, or at least, the size of the market's moves shrinking. Of the 850,000 options traded on VIX, more than 515,000 were puts, with twice as many puts likely initiated by buyers than calls, Cboe data show.

#2753. A trading strategy to ride out the Nvidia uncertainty and generate income in the process

Jensen Huang's abrupt resignation has sent shockwaves through the tech sector, dragging Nvidia's (NVDA) stock down roughly 30% since Oct. 6. The market despises uncertainty, and losing a visionary founder, particularly one without a well-telegraphed succession plan, makes it worse.

However, for opportunistic investors, this panic has made the stock cheaper (obviously) while options have become significantly more expensive. Trading in the $160s, Nvidia's valuation has taken a big haircut, yet its underlying business fundamentals remain largely intact. While Huang was the architect of Nvidia's AI dominance, the company's moat — superior GPUs, the CUDA software ecosystem, and networking expertise — does not walk out the door with him. The company's structural advantage in the global artificial intelligence rollout persists.

Buying good companies at a discount is investing 101, but the current market dynamics may do something else for those who use options.  Huang's departure guarantees that Nvidia will be operating under a cloud of transition for the foreseeable future. Executing a permanent CEO search while navigating global regulatory scrutiny takes time. Consequently, Nvidia's implied volatility (IV)—the market's pricing of expected future price swings — has spiked to highs not seen since the tariff tantrum in the first half of 2025, and this elevated IV is highly likely to persist for several months as the leadership drama slowly unfolds.

This prolonged period of high implied volatility can be a goldmine for option sellers, specifically those executing options overlays such as covered call strategies. When you buy NVDA shares at these depressed levels and simultaneously sell out-of-the-money call options against your position, the premiums you collect are directly tied to IV. Because fear and transition-related uncertainty have dramatically inflated these option prices, the income generated from selling covered calls is now significantly higher than usual.

This lowers the risk profile of holding the stock considerably over time. Consistently collecting these inflated premiums systematically lowers your overall cost basis in the underlying shares. If the stock trades sideways or recovers slowly during the CEO search, you are generating an outsized cash yield on a stagnant asset. If the stock experiences further downside turbulence, the heavy premium income acts as a substantial downside buffer, meaningfully reducing the strategy's overall risk over time.

By deploying a persistent covered call strategy, investors can leverage the exact fear driving the stock down to generate robust, risk-reducing income while maintaining exposure to the undeniable future of AI.

#2752. Saudi national in OpenAI bugging case is out on bond after DOJ tries to block release

Abdulrahman Alfozan, the Saudi national accused of bugging an executive conference room at OpenAI's headquarters, has been released from custody, his attorney told CNBC on Wednesday.

The confirmation of Alfozan's release came a day after a federal judge in San Francisco said the defendant would be allowed out of jail on a $1 million bond, despite prosecutors' warnings that he posed a serious flight risk.

Alfozan, a former OpenAI engineer, is expected to be fitted with an ankle monitor, his attorney, David Rizk, previously told CNBC.

Alfozan was scheduled to be released from detention Wednesday morning at 8 a.m. PT. Shortly before that deadline, U.S. Attorney Craig Missakian of the Northern District of California urged the court to maintain Alfozan's detention pending trial, "given the significant risk that the defendant will flee to Saudi Arabia if he is released from custody."

In an order posted on the case docket later Wednesday, Judge Vince Chhabria said the court only learned of the government's last-minute bid to block Alfozan's release after he had already been let out.The government's motion "is therefore denied as moot," Chhabria's order stated. The judge set a hearing for Dec. 3 on the bid to revoke the magistrate judge's order releasing Alfozan.

Pointing to Alfozan's "ample resources, significant family and professional ties to Saudi Arabia, and few remaining ties to the United States," Missakian had argued in a court filing that the release conditions imposed by Magistrate Judge Alex Tse "are insufficient to safeguard the defendant's appearance for future proceedings in this case."

Alfozan "presents a serious risk of flight that no set of release conditions can reasonably mitigate," Missakian wrote.

His office sought to pause the magistrate judge's release order until a ruling on a motion to revoke it. The Department of Justice did not immediately respond to CNBC's request for comment on Alfozan's release.

Tse in a court hearing Tuesday ruled in favor of Rizk's motion for his client's release, which said prosecutors have admitted finding "no new evidence to support the theory that Mr. Alfozan was stealing trade secrets, or to support the speculation that he was acting as an unregistered foreign agent for Saudi Arabia."

In a text message to CNBC after that hearing, Rizk said, "We are simply grateful that the Court made the right decision to release Mr. Alfozan and now we look forward to defending the case."

But Missakian's filing Wednesday morning pushed back, saying "the government's investigation of this seven-week-old case is ongoing."

Prosecutors have so far found "hundreds of audio files" on Alfozan's personal computer, "including dozens of recordings of meetings or conversations that occurred at OpenAI throughout September 2026, at least one of which involved representatives of a foreign government and many of which involved discussions of what may be trade secrets," according to the filing.

"To be sure, what the defendant did or intended to do with those audio files remains under investigation, but the absence of that evidence at this point has no bearing" on whether Alfozan is a flight risk, Missakian argued.

Alfozan was arrested Oct. 9 and is accused of concealing a recording device in the artificial intelligence company's conference room to secretly record discussions. The device was found in a USB port in a television in the room on Sept. 30, prosecutors have said.

Jury selection for Alfozan's trial is scheduled to begin Jan. 20.

— CNBC's Dan Mangan contributed to this report.
